✅/2 Generate a coding prompt for this task: Burst/auto-capture trigger via MethodChannel call — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: Android Native (Kotlin)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------->>>>>>>



# NEW: Burst / Auto-Capture Trigger via MethodChannel (CameraX ImageCapture)
# Product: MayasabhaXR — maya-viewer (Flutter client, Android native)
# Scope: New Feature / Native Android (Kotlin) — CameraX ImageCapture
# Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline
# Track: Android Native (Kotlin)
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Add still-capture to the existing CameraX session: a Flutter-triggered **burst** (capture N frames rapidly) and **auto-capture** (capture repeatedly on a cadence until stopped), via a `MethodChannel`. Each captured frame is written out with a precise capture **timestamp** so downstream sensor/pose alignment is possible. This task is the capture TRIGGER + execution + frame output; sensor-pose association, the auto-capture trigger criteria beyond interval/continuous, and any reconstruction are separate tasks in this phase.

This binds an `ImageCapture` use case to the SAME session from the preview task and ideally runs with focus/exposure LOCKED (from the lock task) for frame consistency in 3D/photogrammetry capture.

- [ ] Bind a CameraX `ImageCapture` use case alongside the existing Preview (coordinate with the preview task's binding; mind use-case limits)
- [ ] Expose `captureSingle`, `startBurst(count, intervalMs?)`, `startAutoCapture(config)`, `stopAutoCapture` over a `MethodChannel`
- [ ] Apply back-pressure: never fire captures faster than the device can drain; serialize / bound in-flight; don't drop silently
- [ ] Record a precise capture timestamp per frame and write frames to a session output location with stable ids
- [ ] Stream progress/results (per-frame + completion + errors) back to Flutter (EventChannel)
- [ ] Coordinate with AF/AE lock so bursts run with stable focus/exposure (recommended)
- [ ] Manage lifecycle: stop auto-capture and cancel in-flight on stop/dispose/background; never capture to a torn-down session

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. The CameraX preview task — where `ProcessCameraProvider.bindToLifecycle` happens; `ImageCapture` must be bound in the SAME bind call / session, and use-case combination limits checked
2. The focus/exposure lock task — to lock AF/AE before/around a burst for frame consistency
3. `android/app/build.gradle` — CameraX `ImageCapture` availability; storage/permissions for output location
4. The capture screen (Flutter) — how it triggers burst/auto-capture and consumes progress
5. App storage conventions — where session frames are written (app-specific dir; no extra storage permission needed for app-scoped files)
6. Existing `MethodChannel`/`EventChannel` conventions — match naming/patterns (likely the camera channel + a capture-events channel)

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Bind ImageCapture
- Add an `ImageCapture` use case to the existing session (bound with Preview, and ImageAnalysis if present). Because CameraX limits concurrent use cases per device, verify the Preview + ImageCapture (+ Analysis) combination is supported; if not, degrade or document the constraint.
- Choose capture mode deliberately: `CAPTURE_MODE_MINIMIZE_LATENCY` favors burst throughput; `MAXIMIZE_QUALITY` favors per-frame quality but slows cadence. For photogrammetry bursts, balance throughput vs quality — confirm (default: MINIMIZE_LATENCY for cadence, or capture `ImageProxy` for lower overhead).

### Step 2: Single capture
- `captureSingle` → `takePicture` (file or `OnImageCapturedCallback`), record the capture timestamp (from `ImageInfo`/`ImageProxy`), write to the session output, return the frame id/path/timestamp.

### Step 3: Burst with back-pressure
- `startBurst(count, intervalMs?)` captures `count` frames. Do NOT fire all `takePicture` calls at once — SERIALIZE (capture next only after the prior completes) or bound concurrency to what the device sustains; respect `intervalMs` as a minimum spacing if provided.
- Per frame: record timestamp, write output, emit a progress event. On completion, emit a completion event with the frame list.
- Capacity/latency: if the device can't sustain the requested cadence, slow down rather than queue unboundedly or drop silently; report effective cadence/skips if any.

### Step 4: Auto-capture loop
- `startAutoCapture(config)` runs captures repeatedly until `stopAutoCapture`. Default trigger = interval/continuous (config carries interval/cadence). The motion/angle-delta or stability trigger (using the sensor pipeline) is a SEPARATE integration — expose a hook/config flag but do not implement the sensor criterion here (see Scope).
- The loop must be cancellable promptly; `stopAutoCapture` halts new captures and the loop exits cleanly.

### Step 5: Output + timestamps
- Write frames to a session output directory (app-scoped storage; no extra permission), with stable ids and a deterministic naming scheme. Format = JPEG via `ImageCapture` by default (RAW only if needed + supported — flag).
- Each frame MUST carry a precise capture timestamp (monotonic/sensor-aligned where available, e.g. the image's timestamp) so the sensor-fusion task can align pose to frame. Record it alongside the frame (filename and/or a manifest).

### Step 6: Report to Flutter
- Use an `EventChannel` to stream: per-frame `{ id, path, timestampNs, index, total }`, completion `{ count, sessionId }`, and errors `{ index?, error }`. `MethodChannel` methods start/stop and return immediately (or a started/accepted ack); the heavy work streams via events. Do not block the platform thread on capture.

### Step 7: Lock coordination + lifecycle
- For bursts/auto-capture, ensure AF/AE are locked (call the lock task or document that the capture flow locks first) so frames are consistent; restore/unlock per the capture flow on stop if appropriate.
- On `stopAutoCapture`/dispose/background: stop the loop, cancel in-flight captures where possible, flush/close outputs, and never call `takePicture` on an unbound session.

## API / Channel Contract

```
MethodChannel: "mayasabhaxr/camera_capture" (or the app's camera channel)
  captureSingle()                       -> { id, path, timestampNs }
  startBurst(count, intervalMs?)        -> ack (frames stream via EventChannel)
  startAutoCapture({ intervalMs, ... }) -> ack (runs until stop)
  stopAutoCapture()                     -> void

EventChannel: "mayasabhaxr/camera_capture_events"
  frame:      { id, path, timestampNs, index, total? }
  completed:  { count, sessionId }
  error:      { index?, message }

Guarantees: operates on the existing bound session; back-pressured (no silent drops/unbounded queue);
each frame timestamped; cancellable; off the platform thread.
```

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT rebind/recreate the camera — bind `ImageCapture` into the existing session (coordinate with preview).
- Do NOT fire captures faster than the device can drain — serialize/bound; no silent drops or unbounded queue.
- Do NOT lose per-frame timestamps — downstream sensor alignment depends on them.
- Do NOT block the main/platform thread on `takePicture`; stream results via the EventChannel.
- Do NOT capture to a torn-down/unbound session; cancel in-flight and stop the loop on stop/dispose/background.
- Do NOT implement the sensor/motion auto-trigger criterion or pose association here — separate tasks (expose a hook only).
- Do NOT implement reconstruction/processing here.
- Do NOT request CAMERA permission or manage core session lifecycle — preview task / P2.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Burst requested while a capture is in-flight → queued/serialized, not overlapping uncontrollably.
- [ ] `stopAutoCapture` mid-loop / mid-burst → halts promptly, cancels in-flight where possible, no orphaned captures.
- [ ] A single frame fails (write error / capture error) → reported via error event; burst continues or aborts per the documented policy (default: report and continue, mark the gap).
- [ ] Storage full / write failure → graceful error event, no crash.
- [ ] Device cannot bind Preview + ImageCapture (+ Analysis) together → handled/degraded with a clear report.
- [ ] App backgrounded during auto-capture → loop stops; resumes only on explicit restart.
- [ ] Requested cadence exceeds device capability → effective cadence slows; reported, not silently dropped.
- [ ] `count = 0` or invalid config → validated, no-op/error, no crash.
- [ ] Very large burst → bounded memory/disk; outputs streamed/flushed, not all held in memory.

## Constraints

- `ImageCapture` MUST bind into the existing session; no rebind/recreate; use-case combination verified.
- Capture MUST be back-pressured: serialized/bounded, no silent drops or unbounded queue; effective cadence reported when throttled.
- Every frame MUST carry a precise capture timestamp recorded with the output.
- Start/stop MUST be async (ack + EventChannel stream); the platform thread MUST NOT block on capture.
- Auto-capture MUST be promptly cancellable and MUST stop on dispose/background.
- The sensor/motion trigger criterion and pose association MUST NOT be implemented here (hook only).
- No permission/session-lifecycle/reconstruction work here.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] `captureSingle` writes one timestamped frame and returns its id/path/timestamp.
- [ ] `startBurst(count)` produces `count` timestamped frames, streamed via events, without overlapping captures or silent drops; completion event fires.
- [ ] `startAutoCapture`/`stopAutoCapture` runs and halts a continuous capture loop cleanly; stop cancels in-flight.
- [ ] Requested cadence beyond device capability slows gracefully and is reported (no unbounded queue, no silent loss).
- [ ] Each frame has a precise, recorded capture timestamp suitable for later sensor alignment.
- [ ] A per-frame failure is reported via the error event and handled per policy (default: continue, mark gap).
- [ ] Backgrounding during auto-capture stops the loop; the camera session is never captured-to while unbound.
- [ ] Preview + ImageCapture coexist on the device (or the unsupported combination is reported/degraded).
- [ ] Bursts run with AF/AE locked (coordination with the lock task) for frame consistency.
- [ ] Start/stop are async; no platform-thread blocking or ANR during capture.
- [ ] Builds clean; the Flutter wrapper passes `flutter analyze`.

## Testing Instructions

1. With preview running and AF/AE locked, call `startBurst(10)` → confirm 10 timestamped frames written, streamed in order, completion event fires, no overlap/drops.
2. Call `captureSingle` → confirm one frame + timestamp returned.
3. `startAutoCapture` for a few seconds then `stopAutoCapture` → confirm continuous frames then a clean stop; confirm no captures after stop.
4. Request an unrealistically high cadence → confirm it throttles to device capability and reports effective cadence (no unbounded queue, no silent loss).
5. Fill storage / force a write error on one frame → confirm an error event and the documented continue/abort behavior; no crash.
6. Background the app during auto-capture → confirm the loop stops and the session isn't captured-to while unbound.
7. On a device that can't bind Preview+ImageCapture(+Analysis) → confirm the reported/degraded handling.
8. Verify each frame's recorded timestamp is precise and monotonic (suitable for sensor alignment).
9. Profile during burst → confirm no main/platform-thread blocking/ANR.
10. Build + `flutter analyze` → confirm clean.

## Assumptions

- **Builds on preview + lock:** Assumes the preview task owns the session and `ImageCapture` binds there; assumes the lock task can lock AF/AE before a burst. Blocked on preview if absent; lock coordination is recommended for consistency.
- **Auto-trigger criterion:** Defaulted to interval/continuous. The motion/angle-delta or stability-based auto-trigger (using the sensor pipeline) is a SEPARATE task; this exposes a hook/config but does not implement the sensor criterion.
- **Output:** Assumed JPEG via `ImageCapture` written to app-scoped session storage with timestamps + a manifest. RAW only if the pipeline needs it and the device supports it — confirm. Pose/sensor association is the sensor-fusion task's job; this task guarantees the timestamp.
- **Capture mode:** Assumed a throughput-favoring mode (or `ImageProxy` path) for burst cadence; confirm the quality/throughput balance the reconstruction needs.
- **Use-case limits:** Assumes Preview + ImageCapture is bindable; if Analysis is also needed concurrently, verify the device supports the combination and document fallbacks.
- **Transport:** Assumed `MethodChannel` (start/stop) + `EventChannel` (frame/progress/error stream). Match the app's conventions.
- **Lifecycle/permission:** Owned by the preview task / P2; this task neither requests permission nor manages core session lifecycle.
- **Stack:** Kotlin + CameraX `ImageCapture` + Flutter `MethodChannel`/`EventChannel`. Adjust to the app's structure.

































[[[[[[[[[[[ 10 ]]]]]]]]]]]

Generate a coding prompt for this task: JPEG capture with consistent resolution policy (configurable) — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: Android Native (Kotlin)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------>>>

# NEW: JPEG Capture with Consistent, Configurable Resolution Policy
# Product: MayasabhaXR — maya-viewer (Flutter client, Android native)
# Scope: New Feature / Native Android (Kotlin) — CameraX ImageCapture resolution policy
# Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline
# Track: Android Native (Kotlin)
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Govern the resolution and JPEG quality of captured stills so that frames are CONSISTENT within a session and PREDICTABLE across devices, driven by a configurable policy. For 3D/photogrammetry capture, the reconstruction pipeline needs known, uniform input dimensions; CameraX otherwise picks per-device sizes that vary. This task defines and applies a configurable resolution policy on the `ImageCapture` use case (bound in the preview/burst session), reports the ACTUAL chosen resolution, and keeps aspect ratio consistent with the preview/analysis streams.

Key constraint: CameraX `ResolutionSelector` is applied at BIND time. "Configurable" therefore means configured at session start; changing the policy mid-session requires rebinding the use case (owned by the preview/session task). This task does not capture frames (the burst task does) — it parameterizes how those frames are sized and encoded.

- [ ] Define a configurable resolution policy (target size or target long-edge + aspect ratio + fallback rule)
- [ ] Apply it via `ResolutionSelector` on the `ImageCapture` use case at bind time
- [ ] Map the target to the closest SUPPORTED size deterministically when the exact target isn't available
- [ ] Report the ACTUAL resolution chosen (may differ from target) back to Flutter
- [ ] Make JPEG quality configurable and consistent across the session
- [ ] Keep capture aspect ratio consistent with preview/analysis (no FOV mismatch)
- [ ] Ensure all frames in a session share the same resolution + quality

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. The burst/capture task + the preview task — where `ImageCapture` is built and bound; the `ResolutionSelector` and `jpegQuality` are set there, so this policy flows into that bind
2. `android/app/build.gradle` — CameraX version (confirm `ResolutionSelector`/`ResolutionStrategy` API availability; it replaces the deprecated `setTargetResolution`/`setTargetAspectRatio`)
3. The preview/analysis aspect-ratio choice — capture aspect ratio must match to keep FOV consistent across streams
4. App/remote config — where the resolution policy config originates (Flutter arg, app config, or `GET /remote-config` from P1)
5. The capture screen (Flutter) — how it passes the policy and reads back the actual resolution
6. Existing `MethodChannel` conventions

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Define the policy config
A configurable policy supplied at session start, e.g.:
```
ResolutionPolicy {
  // one of:
  targetWidth/targetHeight   // exact target, OR
  targetLongEdge             // e.g. 3000 px on the long edge
  aspectRatio                // e.g. 4:3 or 16:9 — must match preview/analysis
  fallbackRule               // closest-higher-then-lower | closest-lower | none/exact
  jpegQuality                // 1..100
}
```
Validate inputs (positive sizes, quality in range, a supported aspect ratio). Provide sensible defaults.

### Step 2: Build the ResolutionSelector
- Construct a `ResolutionSelector` with a `ResolutionStrategy` from the target size + fallback rule (e.g. `FALLBACK_RULE_CLOSEST_HIGHER_THEN_LOWER`) and an `AspectRatioStrategy` matching the preview/analysis aspect ratio.
- Optionally use a `ResolutionFilter` for custom ordering if the policy needs finer control (e.g. prefer exact long-edge).
- Apply it on `ImageCapture.Builder().setResolutionSelector(...)`, and `setJpegQuality(quality)`, and the target rotation (so EXIF orientation is correct and consistent).

### Step 3: Determinism + closest-supported mapping
- The exact target may be unsupported. The fallback rule MUST deterministically select the closest supported size (same choice on the same device every time), and the policy MUST behave predictably across devices (e.g. always honor the long-edge/aspect intent within device limits).
- If the target exceeds the device max, clamp to the device max (don't fail); if below the min, clamp up — per the rule.

### Step 4: Report the actual resolution
- After bind, read the `ImageCapture` resolution info (the actual output size CameraX selected) and report `{ width, height, jpegQuality, aspectRatio }` back to Flutter. The pipeline/UI must know the real dimensions, since they can differ from the target on constrained devices. Do NOT silently diverge without reporting.

### Step 5: Session consistency
- Because resolution is set at bind, all frames captured in that session share the same resolution and quality automatically — ensure nothing changes it mid-session. Reconfiguring the policy requires a rebind (document this; the rebind is the session task's responsibility).
- Keep the capture aspect ratio equal to preview/analysis so cropping/FOV is consistent across streams.

### Step 6: Expose config + reporting to Flutter
`MethodChannel` (per app convention; likely the camera channel):
- The policy is supplied at session start (with start/bind), or via `configureCaptureResolution(policy)` that takes effect on the next bind.
- `getActiveCaptureResolution()` → `{ width, height, jpegQuality, aspectRatio, target?, fellBack: bool }` so Flutter knows the effective size and whether a fallback occurred.

## API / Config Contract

```
ResolutionPolicy (supplied at session start / bind):
  { targetWidth?, targetHeight?, targetLongEdge?, aspectRatio, fallbackRule, jpegQuality }

MethodChannel: "mayasabhaxr/camera_capture" (or app camera channel)
  configureCaptureResolution(policy)      -> ack (applies on next bind)
  getActiveCaptureResolution()            -> { width, height, jpegQuality, aspectRatio, target?, fellBack }

Guarantees: resolution/quality set at bind; consistent across a session; deterministic closest-supported
selection; actual resolution reported; aspect ratio matches preview/analysis.
Note: changing the policy requires a rebind (session task).
```

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT assume the exact target resolution is supported — use a deterministic fallback rule and clamp to device min/max.
- Do NOT silently produce a resolution different from the target without REPORTING the actual (and a `fellBack` flag).
- Do NOT expect a mid-session resolution change to apply without a rebind — `ResolutionSelector` is bind-time.
- Do NOT let capture aspect ratio differ from preview/analysis — FOV/crop must be consistent.
- Do NOT use the deprecated `setTargetResolution`/`setTargetAspectRatio` if `ResolutionSelector` is available.
- Do NOT hardcode a single device's resolution — the policy must be device-adaptive yet deterministic.
- Do NOT capture frames or do processing here — this configures sizing/encoding only (burst task captures).
- Do NOT manage permission or core session lifecycle — preview task / P2.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Target resolution unsupported → closest supported per the fallback rule; `fellBack: true`; actual reported.
- [ ] Target exceeds device max → clamp to max; report actual.
- [ ] Target below device min → clamp up per rule; report actual.
- [ ] Requested aspect ratio unsupported → nearest supported; keep it equal to preview/analysis; report.
- [ ] Invalid config (zero/negative size, quality out of 1..100, bad aspect) → validated/rejected with defaults or error, no crash.
- [ ] Reconfigure requested mid-session → documented as requiring a rebind; takes effect on next bind, not immediately.
- [ ] Device with very limited supported sizes → deterministic pick; reported.
- [ ] Portrait vs landscape target / rotation → EXIF orientation consistent; reported dimensions reflect the convention used.
- [ ] All burst frames in a session → verified identical resolution + quality.

## Constraints

- Resolution + JPEG quality MUST be set at bind via `ResolutionSelector` + `setJpegQuality`; consistent for the whole session.
- The target → supported-size mapping MUST be deterministic (same device → same result) and predictable across devices.
- The ACTUAL chosen resolution MUST be reported (with a fallback flag); never silently diverge.
- Capture aspect ratio MUST match preview/analysis.
- Policy changes MUST require/await a rebind (documented); no illusory mid-session change.
- Config MUST be validated; invalid input fails safe (defaults or error), never crashes.
- MUST stay scoped to sizing/encoding — no capture triggering, processing, permission, or lifecycle work.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A configured target resolution + JPEG quality is applied to captured JPEGs at session start.
- [ ] When the exact target is unsupported, the closest supported size is chosen deterministically and `getActiveCaptureResolution` reports the actual size with `fellBack: true`.
- [ ] Targets above/below device limits are clamped and reported; invalid configs fail safe.
- [ ] All frames in a single burst/session share the same resolution and JPEG quality (verified).
- [ ] Capture aspect ratio matches preview/analysis (no FOV mismatch).
- [ ] `getActiveCaptureResolution` returns accurate `{ width, height, jpegQuality, aspectRatio, fellBack }`.
- [ ] Reconfiguring the policy takes effect only after a rebind (documented), not silently mid-session.
- [ ] Deprecated resolution APIs are not used where `ResolutionSelector` is available.
- [ ] EXIF/orientation is consistent across frames.
- [ ] Builds clean; the Flutter wrapper passes `flutter analyze`.

## Testing Instructions

1. Configure a common target (e.g. long-edge 3000, 4:3, quality 90) and start a session → capture frames → confirm all frames are the same resolution and quality, matching (or closest-supported to) the target.
2. On a device that doesn't support the exact target → confirm a deterministic closest-supported choice and that `getActiveCaptureResolution` reports the actual with `fellBack: true`.
3. Configure a target above the device max → confirm clamping to max and accurate reporting.
4. Configure an invalid policy (quality 200, zero size) → confirm safe handling (defaults/error), no crash.
5. Confirm capture aspect ratio equals the preview/analysis aspect ratio (no FOV/crop mismatch) on two devices with different sensors.
6. Request a policy change mid-session → confirm it does NOT change current-session frames and applies only after a rebind.
7. Inspect captured JPEG EXIF/orientation across frames → confirm consistency.
8. Run on two differing devices → confirm the policy yields predictable, reported results on both.
9. Build + `flutter analyze` → confirm clean.

## Assumptions

- **Builds on capture/preview:** Assumes `ImageCapture` is built/bound in the burst/preview task; this policy is applied there at bind. Blocked on those if absent.
- **ResolutionSelector available:** Assumes a CameraX version with `ResolutionSelector`/`ResolutionStrategy`/`AspectRatioStrategy`. If on an older CameraX, document the deprecated fallback but prefer upgrading.
- **Policy source:** Assumed the policy comes from Flutter at session start (or app/remote config, possibly tied to `GET /remote-config`). Confirm whether it should be remotely tunable.
- **Bind-time constraint:** Assumes resolution changes require a rebind; the session/preview task owns rebinding. Confirm the UX for changing resolution (likely only between sessions).
- **Aspect ratio:** Assumes capture must match preview/analysis aspect ratio; confirm the canonical aspect ratio for the pipeline.
- **Format:** JPEG (per the task). RAW/other formats are out of scope; if the pipeline later needs RAW, that's a separate policy.
- **Scope:** Sizing/encoding policy only — capture triggering is the burst task; pose/sensor association and processing are separate; permission/lifecycle are preview/P2.
- **Stack:** Kotlin + CameraX `ImageCapture`/`ResolutionSelector` + Flutter `MethodChannel`. Adjust to the app's structure.





























[[[[[[[[[[[[[[ 9 ]]]]]]]]]]]]]]

Generate a coding prompt for this task: EXIF extraction + attach capture metadata (timestamp, device, resolution) — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: Android Native (Kotlin)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------>>>


# NEW: EXIF Extraction + Attach Capture Metadata (timestamp, device, resolution)
# Product: MayasabhaXR — maya-viewer (Flutter client, Android native)
# Scope: New Feature / Native Android (Kotlin) — EXIF + sidecar metadata
# Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline
# Track: Android Native (Kotlin)
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Make each captured JPEG self-describing for the reconstruction pipeline: EXTRACT the EXIF that CameraX/the device already wrote, and ATTACH capture metadata — precise timestamp, device descriptor, and actual resolution (plus camera intrinsics and orientation where available). Standard, interoperable fields go into EXIF; richer/app-specific and high-precision fields go into a per-frame JSON SIDECAR manifest.

The defining distinction: EXIF `DateTime` is wall-clock at 1-second resolution and is for human/interop use only. The pipeline's sensor-alignment-grade timestamp is the precise monotonic timestamp recorded at capture (by the burst task) — it lives in the sidecar, NOT in EXIF datetime. Do not conflate them.

This runs as a per-frame post-capture step coordinating with the burst task (precise timestamp) and the resolution-policy task (actual resolution). Pose/sensor association is a separate task — reserve a sidecar slot for it; do not compute pose here.

- [ ] Read existing EXIF from each captured JPEG (orientation, datetime, make/model, focal length, etc.)
- [ ] Write/normalize standard interop EXIF (orientation, make/model, software, intrinsics if known)
- [ ] Write a per-frame JSON sidecar with the precise monotonic timestamp, device descriptor, actual resolution, capture settings, and a reserved pose slot
- [ ] Strip/omit GPS/location by default (privacy)
- [ ] Do EXIF/sidecar I/O off the main thread without stalling the burst cadence
- [ ] Never corrupt the JPEG when writing EXIF (safe write)

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. The burst/capture task — the precise per-frame capture timestamp and the frame output paths/ids this task annotates; integrate as a per-frame post-capture step
2. The resolution-policy task — the ACTUAL resolution chosen (for the sidecar/EXIF) and aspect ratio
3. The focus/exposure-lock task — lock state / exposure settings to record in the sidecar (capture conditions)
4. `android/app/build.gradle` — `androidx.exifinterface:exifinterface` dependency
5. The session output location convention — where frames + sidecars are written (sidecar naming relative to each frame)
6. `CameraCharacteristics` access (from the preview/lock tasks) — focal length, sensor physical size, lens info for intrinsics
7. Existing JSON/serialization conventions for the sidecar

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Extract existing EXIF
- For each captured JPEG, open `androidx.exifinterface.media.ExifInterface` and read what the device/CameraX wrote: `TAG_ORIENTATION`, `TAG_DATETIME`, `TAG_MAKE`/`TAG_MODEL`, `TAG_FOCAL_LENGTH`, `TAG_F_NUMBER`, `TAG_EXPOSURE_TIME`, `TAG_PHOTOGRAPHIC_SENSITIVITY` (ISO), `TAG_IMAGE_WIDTH`/`TAG_IMAGE_LENGTH`, etc.
- Use these as a baseline; do not assume all are present (varies by device).

### Step 2: Write/normalize standard EXIF (interop)
- Ensure interoperable tags are set/consistent: orientation (normalized to the pipeline's convention), `TAG_MAKE`/`TAG_MODEL`, `TAG_SOFTWARE` (app id/version), `TAG_DATETIME`/`TAG_DATETIME_ORIGINAL` (+ `TAG_SUBSEC_TIME*` if used) for human/interop time, and camera intrinsics (`TAG_FOCAL_LENGTH`, `TAG_FOCAL_LENGTH_IN_35MM_FILM`, `TAG_F_NUMBER`) when known — intrinsics are valuable for photogrammetry calibration.
- Save safely (see Step 5). Keep EXIF for interop; do NOT try to cram arbitrary app data into EXIF.

### Step 3: Write the per-frame sidecar manifest (app/pipeline)
Write a JSON sidecar per frame (e.g. `<frame>.json`) containing the fields EXIF can't hold well or precisely:
```json
{
  "sessionId": "...",
  "frameId": "...",
  "frameIndex": 0,
  "captureTimestampNs": 123456789012345,   // PRECISE monotonic (from burst task) — for sensor alignment
  "wallClockIso": "2026-06-17T10:00:00.123Z", // human/interop, distinct from the precise ts
  "device": { "manufacturer": "...", "model": "...", "osVersion": "...", "appVersion": "...", "cameraId": "..." },
  "resolution": { "width": 4000, "height": 3000, "aspectRatio": "4:3", "jpegQuality": 90, "fellBack": false },
  "intrinsics": { "focalLengthMm": 4.2, "focalLength35mm": 26, "fNumber": 1.8, "sensorWidthMm": 6.4, "sensorHeightMm": 4.8 },
  "capture": { "afLocked": true, "aeLocked": true, "exposureTimeNs": null, "iso": null, "focusDistanceDiopters": null },
  "orientationApplied": "...",
  "pose": null   // RESERVED — filled by the sensor/pose-fusion task; not computed here
}
```
The precise `captureTimestampNs` is the alignment key; the sidecar is the source of truth for app/pipeline metadata.

### Step 4: GPS / privacy
- By default, DO NOT write GPS/location into EXIF or the sidecar (the app has no location permission and capture location is typically irrelevant + a privacy concern). If any GPS exists in source EXIF, strip it unless the pipeline explicitly requires it (confirm).

### Step 5: Safe, non-corrupting writes
- `ExifInterface.saveAttributes()` rewrites the JPEG; a failure mid-write can corrupt the file. Write safely: operate on a copy/temp and atomically replace, or ensure the library's safe path is used; verify the file is still a valid JPEG after writing.
- Read back key tags after writing to confirm they persisted (at least in tests).

### Step 6: Threading + cadence
- All EXIF/sidecar I/O runs off the main thread and off the capture cadence path so it does NOT stall the burst (the burst task streams frames; metadata attachment happens asynchronously per frame, e.g. on an I/O executor, possibly slightly behind the capture without blocking new captures).
- Ensure every frame eventually gets its EXIF + sidecar even under a fast burst (queue/process; report failures).

## Output / Manifest Contract

```
Per captured frame:
  <frame>.jpg   — with normalized interop EXIF (orientation, make/model, software, datetime, intrinsics)
  <frame>.json  — sidecar: sessionId, frameId/index, captureTimestampNs (precise, alignment key),
                  wallClockIso, device, resolution(actual), intrinsics, capture settings,
                  orientationApplied, pose:null (reserved)

Rules:
  - EXIF datetime = human/interop (1s); precise alignment timestamp = sidecar captureTimestampNs.
  - No GPS/location by default.
  - JPEG never corrupted by EXIF writes.
  - Metadata I/O off-main-thread; never stalls burst cadence.
  - pose left null for the sensor/pose task.
```

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT use EXIF wall-clock datetime as the sensor-alignment timestamp — that's the sidecar `captureTimestampNs`.
- Do NOT cram arbitrary app data into EXIF — use the sidecar for structured/app/pipeline metadata.
- Do NOT write GPS/location by default; strip incoming GPS unless explicitly required.
- Do NOT corrupt the JPEG on EXIF write — use a safe/atomic write and validate.
- Do NOT block the main thread or stall the burst cadence on metadata I/O.
- Do NOT compute or attach camera pose here — reserve the sidecar slot; that's the sensor/fusion task.
- Do NOT do reconstruction/processing here; do NOT manage permission or session lifecycle.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] JPEG with minimal/absent EXIF from the device → write what's known; omit unknown intrinsics gracefully.
- [ ] Device that doesn't expose intrinsics (focal length/sensor size) → omit those fields; do not fabricate.
- [ ] EXIF write failure → JPEG not corrupted (temp + atomic replace); error reported; sidecar still written.
- [ ] Orientation tag present vs absent → normalize to the pipeline convention; record what was applied.
- [ ] Fast burst → every frame still gets EXIF + sidecar (async queue), without slowing capture; failures reported, not silently skipped.
- [ ] Incoming GPS in source EXIF → stripped by default.
- [ ] Sub-second precision needed for human time → use SUBSEC tags for EXIF; precise ns stays in the sidecar regardless.
- [ ] Sidecar write failure (disk) → reported; the frame is flagged as missing metadata, not silently lost.

## Constraints

- Standard interop fields → EXIF; app/pipeline + precise timestamp → sidecar JSON. Do not conflate datetime with the alignment timestamp.
- The precise monotonic `captureTimestampNs` (from the burst task) MUST be recorded per frame for sensor alignment.
- Actual resolution (from the policy task), device descriptor, intrinsics (when known), and capture settings MUST be recorded.
- GPS/location MUST be omitted/stripped by default.
- EXIF writes MUST be safe (no JPEG corruption) and verified.
- Metadata I/O MUST be off-main-thread and MUST NOT stall the burst cadence; every frame eventually annotated.
- Pose MUST be left as a reserved null slot for the sensor/fusion task.
- Scoped to metadata — no capture triggering, processing, permission, or lifecycle work.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Each captured JPEG has normalized interop EXIF (orientation, make/model, software, datetime, intrinsics when known).
- [ ] Each frame has a sidecar JSON with `captureTimestampNs` (precise, matching the burst task), device descriptor, actual resolution, intrinsics/capture settings, and `pose: null`.
- [ ] The precise alignment timestamp is in the sidecar, distinct from the EXIF wall-clock datetime.
- [ ] No GPS/location is written; incoming GPS is stripped by default.
- [ ] EXIF writing never corrupts the JPEG (validated readable after write); read-back confirms tags persisted.
- [ ] Under a fast burst, every frame gets EXIF + sidecar without stalling capture; failures are reported, not silently dropped.
- [ ] Devices lacking intrinsics omit those fields gracefully (no fabricated values).
- [ ] Orientation is normalized and the applied orientation is recorded.
- [ ] Metadata I/O runs off the main thread (no jank/ANR during capture).
- [ ] Pose is reserved (null) for the sensor task; no pose computed here.
- [ ] Builds clean; any Flutter wrapper passes `flutter analyze`.

## Testing Instructions

1. Capture a frame → read its EXIF → confirm orientation, make/model, software, datetime, and intrinsics (if the device exposes them) are present and normalized.
2. Read the sidecar → confirm `captureTimestampNs` matches the burst task's recorded timestamp (precise, monotonic), and device/resolution/intrinsics/capture fields are correct; `pose` is null.
3. Confirm the EXIF datetime and the sidecar precise timestamp are DISTINCT representations (interop vs alignment).
4. Confirm no GPS tags are written; seed a source JPEG with GPS → confirm it's stripped.
5. Force an EXIF write failure → confirm the JPEG remains a valid, readable image and the error is reported; sidecar still written.
6. Run a fast burst → confirm every frame gets EXIF + sidecar, capture cadence is not stalled, and any failures are reported (not silently missing).
7. On a device lacking intrinsics → confirm those fields are omitted, not fabricated.
8. Profile during capture → confirm metadata I/O is off the main thread.
9. Build + `flutter analyze` → confirm clean.

## Assumptions

- **Builds on capture/resolution/lock:** Assumes the burst task provides the precise per-frame timestamp + frame paths, the resolution-policy task provides the actual resolution, and the lock task provides capture settings. This task consumes and annotates; blocked on the burst task if absent.
- **EXIF + sidecar split:** Assumes interop EXIF + a JSON sidecar is the chosen model (EXIF can't hold precise ns timestamps or arbitrary structured data well). Confirm the sidecar format/location convention.
- **Intrinsics:** Assumes focal length / sensor size come from `CameraCharacteristics` when available; omitted (not fabricated) when not. Intrinsics are valuable for photogrammetry calibration — include when present.
- **GPS/privacy:** Assumed no location is written and incoming GPS is stripped (no location permission; privacy). Confirm if the pipeline ever needs geotags.
- **Pose:** Reserved null slot; the sensor/pose-fusion task fills it later using the precise timestamp as the join key. Not computed here.
- **Timestamp domain:** Assumes the precise timestamp is the monotonic capture time the burst task recorded (e.g. image timestamp / elapsedRealtimeNanos), suitable for sensor alignment — consistent with the sensor pipeline's clock domain (a phase-level decision).
- **Safe write:** Assumes a temp+atomic-replace (or library-safe) write to avoid JPEG corruption. Verify read-back.
- **Stack:** Kotlin + `androidx.exifinterface` + JSON sidecar; coordinates with the capture tasks. Adjust to the app's structure.

Generate a coding prompt for this task: IMU: TYPE_ROTATION_VECTOR sampled at 50–100Hz via EventChannel stream — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: Android Native (Kotlin)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------->>>>>>>


# NEW: IMU Rotation Vector Stream (TYPE_ROTATION_VECTOR @ 50–100Hz) via EventChannel
# Product: MayasabhaXR — maya-viewer (Flutter client, Android native)
# Scope: New Feature / Native Android (Kotlin) — SensorManager + EventChannel
# Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline
# Track: Android Native (Kotlin)
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Stream device orientation from the Android `TYPE_ROTATION_VECTOR` sensor to Flutter at 50–100Hz over an `EventChannel`, with each sample carrying a timestamp in a clock domain that ALIGNS with the camera capture timestamps. This is the sensor source the pose/frame-fusion task will later join to captured frames; this task produces the stream, not the alignment.

The dominant correctness concern: **timestamp clock domain.** `SensorEvent.timestamp` is nanoseconds on the boot clock (`elapsedRealtimeNanos`/`CLOCK_BOOTTIME` on modern devices), while CameraX image timestamps default to `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` (`uptimeNanos`) — these differ by deep-sleep time. If the sensor stream and the capture frames are on different clocks, frame↔pose alignment is silently wrong. This task MUST emit timestamps in the phase's agreed clock domain (reconciling with the camera task) and document which clock it uses.

- [ ] Register `TYPE_ROTATION_VECTOR` and stream samples at a configurable 50–100Hz over an `EventChannel`
- [ ] Emit per sample: rotation (quaternion components), accuracy, and a clock-domain-correct `timestampNs`
- [ ] Ensure the sample timestamp aligns with the camera capture clock (reconcile/convert; document the domain)
- [ ] Deliver sensor events off the main thread (HandlerThread) but invoke the `EventSink` on the platform main thread
- [ ] Register on stream-listen, unregister on cancel / stop / background (no leak, no battery drain)
- [ ] Treat the requested rate as a HINT (OS best-effort); report/handle actual delivery
- [ ] Handle absent/unreliable sensor gracefully

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. The CameraX preview/burst tasks — the camera frame TIMESTAMP source and clock; this stream MUST be reconcilable with it (the phase clock-domain decision). If the camera uses `CLOCK_MONOTONIC`, either switch the camera timestamp source to REALTIME/boottime or convert one domain to the other.
2. The EXIF/sidecar task — where `captureTimestampNs` is stored per frame; the fusion task will align this stream to those values, so the clock must match.
3. `MainActivity.kt` / plugin registrant — `EventChannel` registration conventions
4. `android/app/build.gradle` / `minSdkVersion` — sensor APIs; note `HIGH_SAMPLING_RATE_SENSORS` permission only matters above 200Hz (not needed for 50–100Hz)
5. The capture screen (Flutter) — how it starts/stops the stream and consumes samples
6. Existing `EventChannel` usage (e.g. the burst capture-events channel) — match patterns

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Sensor setup
- Get `SensorManager` and the `TYPE_ROTATION_VECTOR` sensor. If absent, report unavailable (some devices have only `TYPE_GAME_ROTATION_VECTOR` or raw gyro — see Assumptions for the sensor-choice tradeoff).
- Register with a microsecond sampling period for the target rate: 100Hz → `samplingPeriodUs = 10_000`; 50Hz → `20_000`. The rate is configurable within 50–100Hz.

### Step 2: Threading (critical)
- Provide a dedicated `HandlerThread` (with its `Handler`) to `registerListener(listener, sensor, periodUs, handler)` so sensor callbacks do NOT run on the main thread at high rate.
- The `EventChannel.EventSink` MUST be invoked on the platform main thread — marshal each emitted sample to main (e.g. post to the main `Handler`) before calling `sink.success(...)`. Calling the sink off the main thread crashes/violates the contract.

### Step 3: Sample payload + timestamp domain
- For each `SensorEvent`: extract the rotation vector quaternion components (values[0..3] = x, y, z, w; `w` may be computed if only 3 values on older sensors) and the accuracy/status.
- Convert/record `event.timestamp` into the AGREED clock domain so it aligns with camera frame timestamps. Document the domain explicitly. If the camera is on a different clock, perform a one-time offset reconciliation (capture a paired reading of both clocks) or switch the camera timestamp source — coordinate with the camera task.
- Emit `{ q: [x,y,z,w], accuracy, timestampNs }`.

### Step 4: Efficient streaming at rate
- 50–100Hz over an `EventChannel` is feasible per-event, but minimize overhead: avoid heavy per-event allocation/boxing; prefer compact encoding (e.g. a typed `double`/`float` list or `ByteData`/`Float64List`-friendly layout). Optionally BATCH N samples and emit periodically to cut channel overhead (trade a little latency for efficiency) — confirm whether per-sample or batched is wanted. Default: per-sample with compact encoding; batching as an option.
- Optionally use `maxReportLatencyUs` for hardware batching if the device supports it and latency tolerance allows.

### Step 5: Lifecycle (register/unregister)
- Register the listener on `EventChannel.onListen` (stream subscribed) and on an explicit start; UNREGISTER on `onCancel`, explicit stop, and app background. A leaked sensor listener drains battery and wastes CPU.
- On background→foreground, re-register only if the stream is still active. Quit/clean up the `HandlerThread` on teardown.
- Guard against double-register and register-after-dispose races.

### Step 6: Accuracy + availability
- Forward sensor accuracy/status changes (`onAccuracyChanged`) so consumers know reliability (rotation vector accuracy depends on magnetometer calibration; low accuracy = unreliable yaw).
- If the sensor is unavailable, emit an error/unavailable event rather than silently producing nothing.

## API / Channel Contract

```
EventChannel: "mayasabhaxr/imu_rotation" (per app convention)
  onListen(args: { rateHz? 50..100 }) -> registers sensor, starts streaming
  onCancel()                          -> unregisters, stops

Sample event:
  { q: [x, y, z, w], accuracy: int, timestampNs: long }   // timestampNs in the agreed (camera-aligned) clock

Optional control (MethodChannel) if needed:
  startImu({ rateHz }) / stopImu()

Guarantees: timestamps align with camera capture clock (documented domain); sensor delivery off-main;
sink called on main; unregistered on cancel/stop/background; requested rate is best-effort.
```

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT emit sensor timestamps in a clock domain that differs from the camera capture timestamps — that breaks frame↔pose alignment. Reconcile/convert and document.
- Do NOT call the `EventChannel.EventSink` off the platform main thread.
- Do NOT receive high-rate sensor callbacks on the main thread — use a `HandlerThread`.
- Do NOT leave the listener registered after cancel/stop/background — unregister to avoid battery drain/leak.
- Do NOT assume the requested Hz is exactly delivered — it's a best-effort hint; rely on the per-sample timestamp, not an assumed cadence.
- Do NOT request `HIGH_SAMPLING_RATE_SENSORS` for 50–100Hz (only needed above 200Hz).
- Do NOT compute pose/frame association here — that's the fusion task; this only produces the stream.
- Do NOT silently produce nothing if the sensor is absent — report unavailable.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Device lacks `TYPE_ROTATION_VECTOR` → report unavailable (and/or document falling back to `GAME_ROTATION_VECTOR` if confirmed); no silent empty stream.
- [ ] OS doesn't honor the requested rate (caps/thermal) → samples arrive at the actual rate; consumers rely on `timestampNs`, not a fixed cadence.
- [ ] Low/uncalibrated accuracy (magnetometer) → accuracy/status forwarded so the consumer can weight/ignore.
- [ ] App backgrounded → unregister; on foreground, re-register only if still subscribed.
- [ ] EventChannel cancel/re-listen churn → register/unregister balanced; no leak, no double-register.
- [ ] Camera on a different clock domain → reconciled (offset or source switch); documented; alignment verified.
- [ ] HandlerThread teardown → quit cleanly on dispose; no callbacks after teardown.
- [ ] Older sensor returning 3 values (no w) → compute w from x,y,z; emit a valid quaternion.

## Constraints

- Sample timestamps MUST be in the phase's agreed clock domain, aligned/reconcilable with camera capture timestamps; the domain MUST be documented.
- Sensor delivery MUST be off the main thread (HandlerThread); the EventSink MUST be invoked on the platform main thread.
- The listener MUST be registered on listen/start and unregistered on cancel/stop/background (no leak/drain).
- The requested 50–100Hz is a best-effort hint; correctness MUST rely on per-sample timestamps, not assumed cadence.
- Streaming MUST be efficient (compact encoding; batching optional) and not jank the UI at rate.
- Sensor absence/unreliability MUST be reported, not silently swallowed.
- MUST stay scoped to the rotation-vector stream — no pose/frame fusion, no other sensors, no capture/permission/lifecycle of the camera.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Subscribing to the EventChannel streams `TYPE_ROTATION_VECTOR` samples at ~50–100Hz with `{ q, accuracy, timestampNs }`.
- [ ] Sample `timestampNs` is in the documented clock domain and aligns with camera capture timestamps (verified by a paired reading).
- [ ] Sensor callbacks run off the main thread; the EventSink is invoked on the main thread; no crashes, no UI jank at rate.
- [ ] The listener unregisters on cancel/stop/background; no battery drain or leak after the stream ends; HandlerThread quits cleanly.
- [ ] Requested rate is treated as best-effort; consumers can reconstruct cadence from timestamps even when the OS caps the rate.
- [ ] Accuracy/status changes are forwarded; low-accuracy is observable to consumers.
- [ ] Absent sensor reports unavailable rather than an empty stream.
- [ ] No `HIGH_SAMPLING_RATE_SENSORS` permission is requested for 50–100Hz.
- [ ] No pose/frame fusion is performed here.
- [ ] Builds clean; the Flutter wrapper passes `flutter analyze`.

## Testing Instructions

1. Subscribe on a device → confirm a steady stream of rotation-vector samples around the requested rate, each with a quaternion, accuracy, and timestamp.
2. Clock alignment: capture a frame (burst task) and a near-simultaneous IMU sample → confirm their timestamps are in the same domain and comparable (the core alignment check). If the camera is on a different clock, confirm the reconciliation/conversion works.
3. Threading: profile at 100Hz → confirm no main-thread jank/ANR; confirm the sink is called on main (no platform-channel threading violation).
4. Lifecycle: cancel the stream / background the app → confirm the listener unregisters (verify via sensor service / battery), and re-subscribing re-registers cleanly.
5. Rate: request 50 and 100Hz → confirm approximate delivery; on a device/condition that caps the rate, confirm timestamps still let you reconstruct actual cadence.
6. Accuracy: move through magnetic interference → confirm accuracy/status changes are forwarded.
7. Absent sensor (or simulate) → confirm an unavailable report, not a silent empty stream.
8. Churn: rapidly subscribe/cancel → confirm balanced register/unregister, no leak.
9. Build + `flutter analyze` → confirm clean.

## Assumptions

- **Clock domain (phase decision):** Assumes the phase fixes a single capture clock domain. `SensorEvent.timestamp` is boot-clock (`elapsedRealtimeNanos`); CameraX defaults to `CLOCK_MONOTONIC`. This task emits in the agreed domain and reconciles with the camera (preferred: set the camera timestamp source to REALTIME/boottime so both match natively; else convert with a measured offset). Confirm the phase clock decision.
- **Sensor choice:** The task specifies `TYPE_ROTATION_VECTOR` (fuses accel+gyro+mag; absolute orientation incl. yaw, but susceptible to magnetic interference). `TYPE_GAME_ROTATION_VECTOR` (accel+gyro; drift-free from magnetic interference but yaw drifts) is often preferred for capture/AR. Honoring the spec (ROTATION_VECTOR); flag the tradeoff and confirm if GAME_ROTATION_VECTOR is preferable for the capture use case.
- **Rate:** 50–100Hz is below the 200Hz `HIGH_SAMPLING_RATE_SENSORS` threshold, so no special permission. Rate is a best-effort hint.
- **Transport:** EventChannel for the stream (start on listen / stop on cancel), optionally a MethodChannel start/stop. Match app conventions.
- **Encoding/batching:** Defaulted to per-sample with compact encoding; batching is an option if channel overhead matters at 100Hz. Confirm latency tolerance.
- **Scope:** Produces the rotation stream only. Pose/frame fusion (joining samples to frames via timestamp) and any other sensors (raw accel/gyro/mag) are separate tasks.
- **Stack:** Kotlin + `SensorManager` + `EventChannel` + a `HandlerThread`. Adjust to the app's structure.
































[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[ 7 ]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]

Generate a coding prompt for this task: Low-pass filter on yaw/pitch/roll to reduce jitter — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: Android Native (Kotlin)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------->>>>>


# NEW: Low-Pass Orientation Filter (smoothed yaw/pitch/roll) to Reduce Jitter
# Product: MayasabhaXR — maya-viewer (Flutter client, Android native)
# Scope: New Feature / Native Android (Kotlin) — sensor signal processing
# Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline
# Track: Android Native (Kotlin)
# Priority: Medium

---

## Task Description

Smooth the device orientation from the IMU rotation-vector stream to reduce high-frequency jitter, producing stable yaw/pitch/roll (and optionally a smoothed quaternion) for UI use (e.g. a capture level/orientation guide). The filter MUST be implemented correctly for rotations — naive scalar low-pass on Euler angles is wrong.

Critical correctness points:
- **Do NOT low-pass yaw/pitch/roll as plain scalars.** Euler angles wrap (averaging 359° and 1° yields 180° — the opposite direction) and suffer gimbal lock near pitch ±90°. Smooth in the QUATERNION domain (slerp/nlerp from the previous smoothed orientation toward the new sample), then derive yaw/pitch/roll from the smoothed quaternion.
- **Be dt-aware.** The IMU rate is best-effort and variable (prior task), so use a time-constant filter: `α = 1 − exp(−dt/τ)` with `dt` from sample timestamps, not a fixed `α` that assumes constant cadence.
- **Handle quaternion double-cover.** `q` and `−q` are the same rotation; before slerp/nlerp, flip the new sample's sign if its dot with the previous smoothed quaternion is negative (take the shortest path), or the output jerks.

This consumes the rotation stream from the IMU task; it does not register sensors itself. It produces a smoothed stream; it does not do pose/frame fusion.

- [ ] Smooth orientation in the quaternion domain (slerp/nlerp), wraparound- and gimbal-safe
- [ ] Derive smoothed yaw/pitch/roll from the smoothed quaternion for output
- [ ] Use a dt-aware time-constant filter (configurable τ / smoothing strength)
- [ ] Handle quaternion double-cover (shortest-path), first-sample init, and large dt gaps
- [ ] Preserve the sample timestamp + clock domain on the smoothed output
- [ ] Run off the main thread; do not assume a fixed sample rate

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. The IMU rotation-vector stream task — the source samples (`{ q:[x,y,z,w], accuracy, timestampNs }`); this filter consumes those (in native, before emit, or as a stage on the same stream)
2. The IMU task's threading model (HandlerThread) — run the filter on that thread, not main
3. The IMU task's timestamp/clock domain — preserve it unchanged on the smoothed output
4. The capture UI (Flutter) consuming smoothed orientation (level/guide) — the output shape it expects
5. App/remote config — whether the smoothing strength (τ) is tunable (possibly via `GET /remote-config`)
6. Existing math utilities (quaternion/rotation helpers) — reuse if present

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Filter in the quaternion domain
- Maintain a `smoothedQ` (the running filtered quaternion). For each incoming sample quaternion `qNew`:
  - Compute `dt` from `timestampNs − prevTimestampNs`.
  - Compute the blend factor `α = 1 − exp(−dt/τ)` (clamped to [0,1]).
  - Double-cover: if `dot(smoothedQ, qNew) < 0`, negate `qNew` (shortest path).
  - `smoothedQ = normalize(slerp(smoothedQ, qNew, α))` (or normalized lerp `nlerp` for cheaper math; nlerp is fine for small per-sample steps at 50–100Hz).
- Normalize `smoothedQ` each step to avoid drift.

### Step 2: Derive yaw/pitch/roll
- Convert `smoothedQ` to a rotation matrix and use `SensorManager.getOrientation` (or a direct quaternion→Euler conversion) to get yaw/pitch/roll. Convert to the units/convention the UI expects (radians vs degrees; document the convention and axis order).

### Step 3: dt-aware, configurable smoothing
- Expose the time constant `τ` (or an equivalent smoothing strength) as configurable. Larger τ = smoother but laggier; smaller τ = more responsive but noisier. Provide a sensible default tuned for a capture guide (smooth but not laggy).
- Because `α` derives from `dt`, smoothing stays consistent even when the IMU delivery rate varies — do NOT hardcode a fixed `α`.

### Step 4: Initialization + gap handling
- First sample: set `smoothedQ = qNew` (no filtering; nothing to blend from).
- Large `dt` gap (dropped samples / app paused / resumed): clamp `dt` (and thus `α`) so a long gap doesn't produce `α≈1` jump-and-also doesn't freeze; consider re-initializing to the new sample if the gap exceeds a threshold (avoid snapping through a long-stale orientation). Define and document the gap policy.

### Step 5: Preserve timestamp + emit
- Emit the smoothed output with the SAME `timestampNs` (and clock domain) as the source sample — do not alter it. Output `{ yaw, pitch, roll, qSmoothed?, timestampNs }`.
- Emit via the IMU EventChannel (as a smoothed variant) or a parallel channel — match the IMU task's transport. Keep encoding compact at rate.

### Step 6: Threading + cost
- Run the filter on the IMU HandlerThread (not main); the math is cheap (a few quaternion ops per sample) but keep allocations minimal at 50–100Hz. Marshal the emitted result to the main thread for the EventSink (per the IMU task's contract).

## Output Contract

```
Input (from IMU task): { q:[x,y,z,w], accuracy, timestampNs }
Filter: quaternion-domain slerp/nlerp, dt-aware α = 1 − exp(−dt/τ), double-cover handled
Output: { yaw, pitch, roll, qSmoothed?, timestampNs }   // same timestamp/clock as input
Config: τ (time constant) configurable; default tuned for a capture guide.
Rules: no scalar Euler low-pass; wraparound/gimbal-safe; rate-independent (dt-based); timestamp preserved.
```

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT low-pass yaw/pitch/roll as independent scalars — wraparound (±180°/0–360°) and gimbal lock make it wrong. Filter the quaternion.
- Do NOT use a fixed smoothing factor assuming constant rate — derive `α` from `dt` (rate varies).
- Do NOT skip the quaternion double-cover sign flip — without it the output jerks on sign flips.
- Do NOT forget to normalize the smoothed quaternion each step (numerical drift).
- Do NOT alter the timestamp/clock domain on the output (downstream alignment depends on it).
- Do NOT over-smooth into noticeable lag for a live guide — tune τ; keep it configurable.
- Do NOT run the filter (or emit) incorrectly threaded — compute off-main, emit on main per the IMU contract.
- Do NOT register sensors or do pose/frame fusion here — consume the IMU stream; fusion is a separate task.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Yaw wraparound (crossing ±180° / 0–360°) → handled correctly via quaternion-domain smoothing (no 180° artifact).
- [ ] Pitch near ±90° (gimbal lock) → quaternion smoothing avoids the singularity; YPR derivation stays stable.
- [ ] Quaternion double-cover (q vs −q) → shortest-path sign flip applied; no jerk.
- [ ] First sample → initialize smoothedQ = sample; no spurious blend.
- [ ] Large dt gap (dropped/paused) → α clamped / re-init per policy; no huge snap or freeze.
- [ ] Variable IMU rate → dt-aware α keeps smoothing consistent regardless of cadence.
- [ ] Extreme config (τ→0 = passthrough, τ→large = very smooth) → behaves sensibly at both ends, no NaN.
- [ ] Numerical drift over long runs → periodic/normalize-each-step keeps the quaternion unit.

## Constraints

- Smoothing MUST be done in the quaternion domain (slerp/nlerp), wraparound- and gimbal-safe; YPR derived from the smoothed quaternion.
- The blend factor MUST be dt-aware (`α = 1 − exp(−dt/τ)`), not a fixed constant; smoothing strength (`τ`) MUST be configurable.
- Quaternion double-cover MUST be handled (shortest path); the smoothed quaternion MUST be normalized each step.
- The output timestamp + clock domain MUST equal the source sample's (unchanged).
- The filter MUST run off the main thread and emit on the main thread per the IMU contract; minimal allocation at rate.
- Large-gap and first-sample behavior MUST be defined; no NaN/snap/freeze at extremes.
- MUST stay scoped to filtering — no sensor registration, no pose/frame fusion.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Smoothed yaw/pitch/roll visibly reduce jitter versus raw, with no wraparound artifact when yaw crosses ±180°/0–360°.
- [ ] Pitch near ±90° does not produce instability (quaternion-domain smoothing).
- [ ] Sign-flipped input quaternions do not cause jerks (double-cover handled).
- [ ] Smoothing is consistent across different/variable IMU delivery rates (dt-aware), verified by comparing steady vs throttled rates.
- [ ] `τ` is configurable; default feels smooth but not laggy for a live guide; extremes don't produce NaN/snap.
- [ ] The output timestamp and clock domain match the input sample exactly.
- [ ] First-sample init and large-gap handling behave per the documented policy (no snap/freeze).
- [ ] The filter runs off the main thread; emission is on main; no jank at 100Hz.
- [ ] No sensor registration or pose fusion is done here.
- [ ] Builds clean; the Flutter wrapper passes `flutter analyze`.

## Testing Instructions

1. Feed recorded/synthetic rotation samples including a yaw sweep across ±180° → assert the smoothed yaw transitions continuously (no 180° flip artifact).
2. Include pitch passing near ±90° → assert no instability/blowup in the derived YPR.
3. Inject a sign-flipped quaternion (q → −q) → assert the smoothed output does not jerk (double-cover handled).
4. Feed samples at steady 100Hz vs throttled/irregular rates with the same τ → assert comparable smoothing (dt-aware), not rate-dependent behavior.
5. Sweep τ from near-zero (passthrough) to large (heavy smoothing) → assert sensible behavior at both ends, no NaN.
6. First sample → assert smoothedQ initializes to it; large dt gap → assert the documented clamp/re-init (no huge snap).
7. Confirm the output timestamp equals the input sample's and is in the same clock domain.
8. Profile at 100Hz → confirm off-main computation, on-main emit, no jank/leak.
9. Build + `flutter analyze` → confirm clean.

## Assumptions

- **Builds on the IMU stream:** Assumes the rotation-vector task provides `{ q, accuracy, timestampNs }`; this filter consumes those (as a native stage before emit, or on the same stream). Blocked on the IMU task if absent.
- **Quaternion-domain filtering:** Assumes smoothing on quaternions (slerp/nlerp) then deriving YPR — the correct, wraparound/gimbal-safe approach. Honoring the "yaw/pitch/roll" output naming while filtering correctly under the hood. nlerp is acceptable at 50–100Hz; slerp if exactness is wanted.
- **dt-aware filter:** Assumes a time-constant `α = 1 − exp(−dt/τ)` so smoothing is rate-independent (the IMU rate varies). τ configurable; possibly tunable via remote config — confirm.
- **Output use:** Assumes the smoothed orientation drives a capture UI guide (level/orientation), so low lag matters. If it instead feeds offline processing, heavier smoothing may be acceptable — confirm.
- **Conventions:** Confirm the YPR units (deg/rad), axis order, and sign conventions the UI expects; document them.
- **Gap policy:** Assumes clamp-dt with re-init beyond a threshold; confirm the threshold and desired behavior on resume.
- **Scope:** Filtering only. Sensor registration is the IMU task; pose/frame fusion is a separate task; this neither registers sensors nor aligns to frames.
- **Stack:** Kotlin + quaternion math on the IMU HandlerThread + the IMU EventChannel transport. Adjust to the app's structure.
