Generate a coding prompt for this task: Stability gate: gyro magnitude < 0.8 rad/s AND accel < 0.15g for 250ms — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: Android Native (Kotlin)

------------------------------------------------------------------------>>>>>>>



# NEW: Stability Gate (gyro < 0.8 rad/s AND linear-accel < 0.15g, sustained 250ms)
# Product: MayasabhaXR — maya-viewer (Flutter client, Android native)
# Scope: New Feature / Native Android (Kotlin) — sensor stability detection
# Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline
# Track: Android Native (Kotlin)
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Detect when the device is held steady enough to capture, and emit a debounced "stable" state that the auto-capture flow can trigger on. The gate is: gyroscope magnitude < 0.8 rad/s AND (gravity-removed) linear-acceleration magnitude < 0.15g, both held CONTINUOUSLY for 250ms. This provides the motion/stability trigger that the burst/auto-capture task deferred to a separate task.

Critical correctness points:
- **"accel < 0.15g" means LINEAR acceleration (gravity removed), not raw accelerometer.** Raw `TYPE_ACCELEROMETER` reads ~1g at rest, so checking its magnitude < 0.15g would NEVER pass and the gate would never open. Use `TYPE_LINEAR_ACCELERATION` (gravity-compensated) and check its magnitude < 0.15g (0.15 × 9.81 ≈ 1.47 m/s²). On devices without linear-acceleration, derive it (accel minus a gravity estimate).
- **The 250ms dwell is measured from sensor timestamps (dt-aware), not an assumed sample rate or wall clock.** The condition must hold continuously; any break resets the dwell timer.
- **Gyro and linear-accel are separate streams arriving independently.** Evaluate the AND on the most-recent value of EACH on every new sample — do not require synchronized samples.

This consumes raw sensors (not the rotation-vector stream) and emits a stability state/trigger; it does NOT perform the capture (the burst task does) and does NOT do orientation smoothing or pose fusion.

- [ ] Register `TYPE_GYROSCOPE` and `TYPE_LINEAR_ACCELERATION` (with gravity-removal fallback if absent)
- [ ] Compute gyro magnitude (rad/s) and linear-accel magnitude (compare in consistent units; 0.15g → m/s²)
- [ ] Evaluate `gyroMag < 0.8 rad/s AND linAccelMag < 0.15g` using most-recent-of-each
- [ ] Require the condition continuously for 250ms (dt-aware dwell; reset on break)
- [ ] Emit a debounced stable/unstable state (+ a "stable" trigger for auto-capture) to Flutter
- [ ] Make thresholds (0.8, 0.15g, 250ms) configurable (possibly via remote config)
- [ ] Correct threading/lifecycle; register/unregister on start/stop/background

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. The IMU rotation-vector task — match the SensorManager + HandlerThread + EventChannel patterns and the timestamp/clock domain (reuse, stay consistent)
2. The burst/auto-capture task — its motion/stability trigger hook; this gate's "stable" event drives it
3. P1 `GET /remote-config` (pitch bands, **thresholds**, segment counts) — these stability thresholds may be the configurable "thresholds"; wire config if so
4. `MainActivity.kt` / plugin registrant — EventChannel registration
5. The capture UI (Flutter) — how a stability indicator / "hold steady" prompt consumes the state
6. Existing math/sensor utilities

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Register sensors
- Register `TYPE_GYROSCOPE` (rad/s, 3-axis) and `TYPE_LINEAR_ACCELERATION` (m/s², gravity removed) at a suitable rate (e.g. SENSOR_DELAY_GAME or a microsecond period), on a HandlerThread (not main).
- If `TYPE_LINEAR_ACCELERATION` is unavailable, fall back: register `TYPE_ACCELEROMETER` and remove gravity (low-pass to estimate gravity, subtract) to get linear accel — document the fallback.

### Step 2: Compute magnitudes + units
- Gyro magnitude = `sqrt(gx² + gy² + gz²)` in rad/s; compare to `0.8` rad/s.
- Linear-accel magnitude = `sqrt(ax² + ay² + az²)` in m/s²; compare to `0.15g = 0.15 × 9.81 ≈ 1.47` m/s². Keep units consistent; convert the g threshold to m/s² (or vice versa) explicitly.

### Step 3: Evaluate the AND on most-recent-of-each
- Hold the latest gyro magnitude and latest linear-accel magnitude. On EACH new sample (from either sensor), re-evaluate `gyroMag < gyroThresh AND linAccelMag < accelThresh`. Do not wait for synchronized pairs; the two streams are independent.

### Step 4: Dwell timer (250ms, dt-aware)
- When the combined condition first becomes true, record the timestamp (sensor timestamp, agreed clock domain). While it remains true, check `now − conditionStartTs ≥ 250ms` → transition to STABLE.
- If the condition becomes false at any point, reset (clear `conditionStartTs`); the dwell restarts on the next true.
- Measure the 250ms from sample timestamps (dt-aware) — do NOT count samples or use wall clock; the rate varies.

### Step 5: Debounced state + trigger
- Emit a STATE: `stable` / `unstable` (and optionally the current magnitudes for a UI indicator). Emit on TRANSITIONS (entered stable / left stable), not every sample, to avoid flooding.
- On entering STABLE, emit a trigger the auto-capture flow consumes (the burst task's hook). Optionally apply hysteresis (separate exit thresholds) to avoid flicker at the boundary — the 250ms dwell already debounces entry; confirm if exit hysteresis is wanted.

### Step 6: Configurable thresholds
- Expose `gyroThresh` (default 0.8 rad/s), `accelThresh` (default 0.15g), and `dwellMs` (default 250) as configurable. If the pipeline tunes these via remote config (P1 "thresholds"), source them there; otherwise from app config / Flutter. Validate ranges.

### Step 7: Threading + lifecycle
- Sensor callbacks on the HandlerThread; emit state transitions to the main thread for the EventSink (per the channel contract).
- Register on start/listen; unregister on stop/cancel/background; quit the HandlerThread on teardown. On resume, reset the dwell (don't carry a stale condition across a pause).

## API / Channel Contract

```
EventChannel: "mayasabhaxr/stability" (per app convention)
  onListen({ gyroThresh?, accelThresh?, dwellMs? }) -> registers sensors, starts evaluating
  onCancel() -> unregister, stop

State event (on transition):
  { stable: bool, gyroMag: double, linAccelMag: double, timestampNs: long }
Trigger event (on entering stable):
  { event: "stable", timestampNs }   // consumed by auto-capture

Rules: linear-accel (gravity removed) for the 0.15g check; AND on most-recent-of-each; 250ms dwell from
sensor timestamps; emit on transitions; thresholds configurable; off-main compute, on-main emit.
```

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT use raw `TYPE_ACCELEROMETER` magnitude for the 0.15g check — gravity (~1g) means it never passes; use `TYPE_LINEAR_ACCELERATION` (or gravity-removed accel).
- Do NOT measure 250ms by counting samples or wall clock — use sensor timestamps (dt-aware); rate varies.
- Do NOT require gyro and accel samples to be synchronized — evaluate the AND on most-recent-of-each.
- Do NOT emit on every sample — emit on stable/unstable transitions (debounced).
- Do NOT let the state flicker at the threshold — the dwell debounces entry; add exit hysteresis if needed.
- Do NOT run sensor work on the main thread; emit transitions on main per the channel contract.
- Do NOT hardcode thresholds if they are meant to be tunable (remote config).
- Do NOT perform the capture here — emit the trigger; the burst task captures.
- Do NOT carry a stale dwell across app pause/resume — reset.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Device already steady at start → dwell accumulates from first satisfied condition; STABLE after 250ms.
- [ ] Brief motion spike during dwell → condition breaks, timer resets, dwell restarts when steady again.
- [ ] `TYPE_LINEAR_ACCELERATION` absent → gravity-removed accel fallback; documented; still ~correct threshold.
- [ ] One sensor missing entirely (no gyro / no accel) → report unavailable/degraded; do not silently never-trigger.
- [ ] Threshold exactly at boundary → define inclusive/exclusive consistently.
- [ ] Rapid stable/unstable toggling → debounced (dwell + optional hysteresis); no event flood.
- [ ] Variable sensor rate → dt-aware dwell stays correct (250ms wall-equivalent regardless of cadence).
- [ ] App pause/resume / large dt gap → reset dwell; don't emit a false STABLE across the gap.
- [ ] Unit mismatch (g vs m/s²) → converted explicitly; no silent factor-of-9.81 bug.

## Constraints

- The 0.15g check MUST use gravity-removed linear acceleration (or a documented gravity-removal fallback), never raw accelerometer magnitude.
- The 250ms dwell MUST be measured from sensor timestamps (dt-aware), continuous, reset on any break.
- The AND MUST be evaluated on the most-recent value of each sensor; no synchronized-sample requirement.
- State MUST be emitted on transitions (debounced), not per sample; entering stable MUST emit the auto-capture trigger.
- Thresholds (gyro, accel, dwell) MUST be configurable (and validated); remote-config-tunable if the pipeline uses it.
- Sensor compute off-main; emit on-main; register/unregister on start/stop/background; reset dwell on resume.
- Units MUST be consistent and conversions explicit.
- MUST stay scoped to stability detection — no capture, no orientation smoothing, no pose fusion.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Holding the device steady produces a STABLE state after ~250ms; moving it produces UNSTABLE.
- [ ] The 0.15g check passes when steady (proving linear/gravity-removed accel is used, not raw accel that would never pass).
- [ ] A motion spike during the dwell resets the timer; stability requires a fresh continuous 250ms.
- [ ] The AND uses most-recent gyro and accel values; the two independent streams don't need to be synchronized.
- [ ] Entering STABLE emits a trigger the auto-capture flow can consume; state events are emitted on transitions, not per sample.
- [ ] Thresholds (0.8 rad/s, 0.15g, 250ms) are configurable and validated; defaults match the spec.
- [ ] dt-aware dwell yields ~250ms regardless of sensor cadence; pause/resume resets and doesn't emit a false STABLE.
- [ ] Linear-accel fallback works on devices lacking `TYPE_LINEAR_ACCELERATION`; missing sensors report degraded, not silent never-trigger.
- [ ] Sensor compute is off-main; emission on-main; listeners unregister on stop/background.
- [ ] No capture/smoothing/fusion performed here.
- [ ] Builds clean; the Flutter wrapper passes `flutter analyze`.

## Testing Instructions

1. Hold the device still on a surface → confirm STABLE within ~250ms; confirm `linAccelMag` is near 0 (gravity removed), proving linear accel is used.
2. Replace linear-accel with raw accelerometer (temporarily) → confirm the gate would never open (demonstrating why linear accel is required), then revert.
3. Introduce a brief shake during the dwell → confirm the timer resets and STABLE requires a fresh 250ms.
4. Move only rotationally (gyro high, accel low) and only translationally (accel high, gyro low) → confirm each independently keeps the gate UNSTABLE (AND of both).
5. Feed steady vs throttled sensor rates → confirm the dwell still equals ~250ms (dt-aware).
6. Tune thresholds via config → confirm behavior changes accordingly; invalid config rejected.
7. Pause/resume the app → confirm dwell resets, no false STABLE across the gap.
8. Simulate `TYPE_LINEAR_ACCELERATION` absent → confirm the gravity-removed fallback still gates correctly.
9. Confirm STABLE entry emits the auto-capture trigger; confirm events are transition-based.
10. Profile → off-main compute, on-main emit, no jank. Build + `flutter analyze` → clean.

## Assumptions

- **Linear acceleration:** Assumes `TYPE_LINEAR_ACCELERATION` (gravity removed) for the 0.15g check; fallback to gravity-removed `TYPE_ACCELEROMETER` if absent. Raw accelerometer magnitude is explicitly NOT used (would never pass).
- **Trigger consumer:** Assumes the burst/auto-capture task consumes the "stable" trigger (its deferred motion/stability hook). This task emits the trigger; it does not capture.
- **Thresholds source:** Defaults 0.8 rad/s, 0.15g, 250ms; configurable, possibly via P1 remote-config "thresholds." Confirm whether remotely tunable.
- **Clock domain:** Uses sensor timestamps in the phase's agreed clock domain (consistent with the IMU task) for the dwell; not wall clock.
- **Hysteresis:** Entry is debounced by the 250ms dwell; exit hysteresis (separate exit thresholds) optional — confirm if flicker on exit is a concern.
- **Sensors needed:** Raw gyroscope + linear acceleration — distinct from the rotation-vector stream (orientation). This task registers its own raw sensors (or shares a sensor manager); coordinate to avoid duplicate registration if the IMU task also reads raw sensors.
- **Scope:** Stability detection + trigger only. Capture is the burst task; orientation smoothing and pose fusion are separate.
- **Stack:** Kotlin + SensorManager (gyro + linear accel) + HandlerThread + EventChannel. Adjust to the app's structure.

























[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[ 5 ]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]

Generate a coding prompt for this task: Blur detection: variance of Laplacian on downscaled grayscale (width 640) — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: Android Native (Kotlin)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------->>>>>


# NEW: Blur Detection — Variance of Laplacian on Downscaled Grayscale (width 640)
# Product: MayasabhaXR — maya-viewer (Flutter client, Android native)
# Scope: New Feature / Native Android (Kotlin) — image-quality processing
# Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline
# Track: Android Native (Kotlin)
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Compute a per-frame sharpness score using the variance of the Laplacian, on a grayscale image downscaled to 640px width, and produce a sharp/blurry decision against a configurable threshold. Blurry frames ruin 3D/photogrammetry reconstruction; this provides the signal the capture flow uses to gate (capture only sharp) or post-filter (reject blurry) frames. This task provides the METRIC + decision; the accept/reject policy lives in the capture flow.

Two correctness anchors:
- **The source grayscale is the luma (Y) plane.** For CameraX `ImageAnalysis` (YUV_420_888), the Y plane IS grayscale — do NOT convert YUV→RGB→gray. BUT read the Y plane respecting `rowStride`/`pixelStride` (the plane is padded; treating it as a contiguous `width×height` buffer reads garbage). For captured JPEGs, decode then take luma.
- **Downscale to a fixed 640px width first.** The raw Laplacian variance scales with resolution; normalizing every frame to 640px width makes ONE threshold valid across source resolutions/devices. Preserve aspect ratio.

This runs off the main thread (on the analyzer/an I/O thread) and must not block the frame pipeline. It does NOT decide capture accept/reject and does NOT do reconstruction.

- [ ] Obtain grayscale: extract the Y (luma) plane (ImageAnalysis), respecting strides; or decode→luma for JPEGs
- [ ] Downscale to 640px width (aspect-preserving) before computing the metric
- [ ] Apply a 3×3 Laplacian and compute the variance of the result
- [ ] Compare to a configurable threshold → sharp/blurry; emit score + decision associated with the frame (id/timestamp)
- [ ] Run off-main; under real-time use, drop frames (keep-latest) rather than stall
- [ ] Make the threshold configurable (possibly remote config); document its content/scale sensitivity

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. The burst/capture + preview tasks — whether blur runs on a CameraX `ImageAnalysis` stream (real-time gating) or on captured JPEGs (post-filter); and the use-case-combination limit (Preview + ImageCapture + ImageAnalysis may exceed device limits)
2. `android/app/build.gradle` — is OpenCV a dependency? If yes, `Laplacian` + `meanStdDev` is convenient; if not, pure Kotlin on the downscaled luma is fine at 640px
3. The EXIF/sidecar task — the sharpness score can be recorded per frame (so the pipeline knows which to reject); the frame id/timestamp join key
4. The stability gate task — pattern for emitting a per-frame quality signal; the capture flow may require BOTH stable AND sharp
5. P1 `GET /remote-config` "thresholds" — the blur threshold may be tunable there
6. Existing image/bitmap utilities

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Get grayscale correctly
- **ImageAnalysis (preferred, real-time):** from the `ImageProxy` (YUV_420_888), read plane[0] (Y/luma). Respect `plane.rowStride` and `plane.pixelStride` — iterate rows using `rowStride`, not `width`, to skip padding. The Y plane gives an 8-bit grayscale image directly; no color conversion.
- **Captured JPEG (post-filter):** decode to a Bitmap and take luma (or decode region/scaled). Avoid full-res decode where possible (use `inSampleSize` to decode near 640px).

### Step 2: Downscale to width 640
- Scale the grayscale image to 640px width, preserving aspect ratio (height = 640 × srcH/srcW). Nearest/bilinear sampling is fine — nearest is fast and adequate for a blur metric. This normalizes the metric scale so the threshold is resolution-independent.

### Step 3: Laplacian + variance
- Apply a 3×3 Laplacian kernel (e.g. 4-connected `[[0,1,0],[1,-4,1],[0,1,0]]` or 8-connected) over the downscaled grayscale.
- Compute the variance of the Laplacian response (`var = mean(L²) − mean(L)²`). This single number is the sharpness score: higher = sharper, lower = blurrier.
- If OpenCV is available, `Laplacian` + `meanStdDev` (variance = stddev²) is equivalent; otherwise a straightforward Kotlin convolution over the 640px buffer is fast (well under a frame at typical rates).

### Step 4: Threshold → decision
- Compare the variance to a configurable threshold: `sharp = variance >= threshold`. Provide a sensible default but expose it as configurable; document that the absolute value is content-sensitive (low-texture/dark scenes yield low variance even when in focus) and scale-normalized by the 640px downscale.

### Step 5: Associate + emit
- Emit `{ frameId/timestampNs, sharpnessScore, sharp, width:640 }` so the pipeline/UI can flag or reject the right frame. For captured frames, record the score in the sidecar (EXIF/metadata task) keyed by frame id; for real-time preview, stream the score (e.g. for a "hold steady / too blurry" indicator).

### Step 6: Performance + backpressure
- Run on the analyzer executor / an I/O thread — never the main thread.
- For real-time `ImageAnalysis`, set `STRATEGY_KEEP_ONLY_LATEST` so slow processing drops frames instead of queuing/stalling. Close each `ImageProxy` promptly (failing to `close()` stalls the analyzer — a classic CameraX bug).
- Keep allocations minimal (reuse buffers) at frame rate.

## API / Output Contract

```
Per frame:
  computeSharpness(frame) -> { sharpnessScore: double, sharp: bool, width: 640, frameId/timestampNs }

Config: blurThreshold (configurable; default tuned at 640px width; content-sensitive)

Rules: grayscale from luma (stride-correct); downscale to 640px width first; variance of 3×3 Laplacian;
off-main; keep-only-latest under real-time; ImageProxy always closed.
```

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT convert YUV→RGB→grayscale when the Y plane is already luma — extract Y directly.
- Do NOT read the Y plane as a contiguous `width×height` buffer — respect `rowStride`/`pixelStride` (padding), or you compute on garbage.
- Do NOT compute the metric on the full-resolution image — downscale to 640px width first (speed + threshold consistency).
- Do NOT run on the main thread; do NOT stall the analyzer — use keep-only-latest and always `close()` the `ImageProxy`.
- Do NOT use deprecated RenderScript.
- Do NOT hardcode the threshold if it's meant to be tunable; document its content/scale sensitivity.
- Do NOT decide capture accept/reject or do reconstruction here — provide the score/decision; the capture flow acts.
- Do NOT lose the frame association (id/timestamp) — the pipeline must reject the correct frame.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] YUV Y plane with `rowStride > width` (padding) → read by stride; correct grayscale (verify on a device with padded strides).
- [ ] Low-texture / dark / uniform scene → low variance even if in focus; documented content sensitivity; threshold tunable (don't claim false precision).
- [ ] Odd aspect ratios → downscale to width 640 preserving aspect; height computed accordingly.
- [ ] Processing slower than frame rate (real-time) → keep-only-latest drops frames; no queue buildup, no stall.
- [ ] `ImageProxy` not closed → analyzer stalls; ensure `close()` in all paths (incl. exceptions).
- [ ] Uniform color image → variance ~0 → classified blurry (expected).
- [ ] Very small source (< 640 wide) → upscaling unnecessary; compute at source width or document handling (don't upscale to inflate the metric).
- [ ] Captured-JPEG path → decode near 640px (`inSampleSize`) to avoid full-res decode cost.

## Constraints

- Grayscale MUST come from luma (Y plane for YUV; luma for JPEG), read stride-correctly; no redundant color conversion.
- The metric MUST be computed on a 640px-width (aspect-preserved) downscaled grayscale, so the threshold is resolution-independent.
- The score MUST be the variance of a 3×3 Laplacian; the decision a comparison to a configurable threshold.
- Output MUST be associated with the frame (id/timestamp) for correct rejection/flagging.
- Processing MUST be off-main; real-time use MUST use keep-only-latest and always close the `ImageProxy`.
- The threshold MUST be configurable (remote-config-tunable if applicable) and its content sensitivity documented.
- MUST stay scoped to the sharpness metric/decision — no capture accept/reject policy, no reconstruction.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A sharp frame yields a high variance and `sharp:true`; a blurry frame yields low variance and `sharp:false`, at the same 640px scale.
- [ ] Grayscale is taken from luma with correct stride handling (verified on a padded-stride device); no YUV→RGB conversion.
- [ ] The metric is computed at 640px width regardless of source resolution, so the threshold behaves consistently across devices/resolutions.
- [ ] The score/decision is associated with the correct frame id/timestamp and recordable in the sidecar / streamable for UI.
- [ ] Under real-time analysis, slow processing drops frames (keep-latest) without stalling; every `ImageProxy` is closed.
- [ ] The threshold is configurable; defaults are sensible at 640px; content sensitivity is documented.
- [ ] Processing runs off the main thread (no jank/ANR at frame rate).
- [ ] No capture accept/reject policy or reconstruction is performed here.
- [ ] Builds clean; any Flutter wrapper passes `flutter analyze`.

## Testing Instructions

1. Feed a known sharp image and a known blurred version (same scene) → confirm sharp has higher variance and crosses the threshold while blurred does not, both at 640px.
2. On a device whose YUV Y plane has `rowStride > width` → confirm correct grayscale (no skew/garbage), proving stride handling.
3. Vary source resolution (e.g. 1080p vs 4000px capture) for the same scene → confirm the 640px-normalized score/threshold behavior is consistent (resolution-independent).
4. Low-texture/dark scene in focus → confirm it may score low (documented content sensitivity); confirm tuning the threshold adjusts behavior.
5. Real-time: artificially slow the analyzer → confirm frames are dropped (keep-latest), no queue/stall; confirm `ImageProxy` is always closed (no analyzer freeze).
6. Confirm score↔frame association (id/timestamp) is correct and recorded in the sidecar (capture path).
7. Profile at frame rate → confirm off-main, no jank; pure-Kotlin path completes well within budget at 640px.
8. Build + `flutter analyze` → confirm clean.

## Assumptions

- **Frame source:** Assumes either a CameraX `ImageAnalysis` YUV stream (real-time gating) or captured JPEGs (post-filter). Confirm which the capture flow needs; if real-time + ImageCapture + Preview, verify the device supports the use-case combination (the recurring use-case-limit concern).
- **Implementation:** Assumes pure Kotlin on the downscaled luma (fast at 640px) unless OpenCV is already a dependency, in which case use `Laplacian`/`meanStdDev`. RenderScript is excluded (deprecated).
- **Threshold:** Default tuned at 640px width; configurable, possibly via P1 remote-config "thresholds." The absolute value is content-sensitive (texture/lighting) — documented, not presented as a universal constant.
- **Use with stability:** Assumes the capture flow may require BOTH stable (stability gate) AND sharp (this) before auto-capture, or use this to reject blurry captured frames. This task provides the signal; the policy is the capture flow's.
- **Clock/association:** Uses the frame id/timestamp (phase clock domain) to associate the score; consistent with the EXIF/sidecar join key.
- **Downscale method:** Assumes aspect-preserving downscale to width 640 (nearest/bilinear). Sources narrower than 640 are handled without upscaling-to-inflate.
- **Scope:** Sharpness metric + decision only. No capture policy, no reconstruction, no sensor work.
- **Stack:** Kotlin + CameraX `ImageAnalysis` (or JPEG decode) + optional OpenCV + EventChannel/sidecar. Adjust to the app's structure.


























[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[[ 4 ]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]]


Generate a coding prompt for this task: Blur thresholds: <40 reject, 40–80 warn, >80 accept — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: Android Native (Kotlin)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------->>>


# NEW: Blur Threshold Policy — <40 reject, 40–80 warn, >80 accept
# Product: MayasabhaXR — maya-viewer (Flutter client, Android native)
# Scope: New Feature / Native Android (Kotlin) — classification policy + config
# Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline
# Track: Android Native (Kotlin)
# Priority: Medium

---

## Task Description

Classify the blur/sharpness score (variance of Laplacian, from the blur-detection task) into three actionable bands using configurable thresholds: REJECT (score < 40), WARN (40 ≤ score ≤ 80), ACCEPT (score > 80). This is the policy layer on top of the metric — the blur-detection task computes the score; this task turns it into a band the capture flow and UI act on.

The two thresholds (40, 80) are content- and device-sensitive magic numbers, so they MUST be configurable (defaults 40/80), ideally tunable via remote config without an app release. The three-band design is intentional: the WARN band is a buffer that avoids a hard sharp/blurry flip at a single cutoff.

- [ ] Map a sharpness score to a band: REJECT / WARN / ACCEPT, with precise boundary semantics
- [ ] Make both thresholds configurable (default 40 and 80; validated)
- [ ] Source thresholds from config (possibly P1 remote-config "thresholds"); apply runtime updates to subsequent classifications
- [ ] Expose the band (and the thresholds used) to the capture flow + UI
- [ ] Keep band SEMANTICS clear (reject = don't use, warn = borderline/flag, accept = use); the capture flow wires the concrete action

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. The blur-detection task — produces the sharpness score this classifies; share the score↔frame association (id/timestamp)
2. P1 `GET /remote-config` ("thresholds") — likely the source for the 40/80 boundaries; wire config if so
3. The capture/auto-capture flow — how it consumes the band (reject → skip/discard, warn → flag/maybe-skip, accept → keep) and any "too blurry" UI prompt
4. The stability gate task — pattern for a configurable, per-frame quality signal feeding capture
5. Existing config/validation utilities

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Define the band classification
```kotlin
enum class BlurBand { REJECT, WARN, ACCEPT }

fun classify(score: Double, rejectBelow: Double, acceptAbove: Double): BlurBand =
    when {
        score < rejectBelow  -> BlurBand.REJECT   // < 40
        score > acceptAbove  -> BlurBand.ACCEPT    // > 80
        else                 -> BlurBand.WARN      // 40 <= score <= 80 (inclusive both ends)
    }
```
Boundary semantics (be precise, matching the spec): `< rejectBelow` → REJECT; `> acceptAbove` → ACCEPT; otherwise WARN. So exactly 40 → WARN and exactly 80 → WARN. Document this inclusivity.

### Step 2: Configurable, validated thresholds
- Expose `rejectBelow` (default 40) and `acceptAbove` (default 80) as config. Validate `rejectBelow <= acceptAbove`. If equal, the WARN band is empty (binary reject/accept) — allowed but note it. Reject invalid config (reject > accept) → clamp/error with a clear message; fall back to defaults rather than producing nonsensical bands.

### Step 3: Config source + runtime updates
- Read thresholds from config; if the pipeline tunes them via remote config (P1 "thresholds"), source there. A runtime threshold update applies to SUBSEQUENT classifications (don't reclassify past frames unless asked). Keep the active thresholds queryable so the band result can report which were used.

### Step 4: Band semantics (policy meaning)
Define what each band MEANS so consumers act consistently:
- REJECT — too blurry; the frame should not be used (discard / don't add to the capture session; in real-time, surface a "too blurry — hold steady" hint).
- WARN — borderline; usable but flagged; the capture flow may accept-with-caution, count differently, or prompt — its choice.
- ACCEPT — sharp enough; use the frame.
This task provides the band + semantics; the capture flow wires the concrete action (skip/keep/prompt). Do not hardcode capture behavior here beyond the semantics.

### Step 5: Expose to consumers
- Provide `classify(score)` (using active thresholds) returning `{ band, score, rejectBelow, acceptAbove }`, associated with the frame id/timestamp from the blur-detection task. Make it available to the capture flow (gating) and the UI (indicator/prompt).

## API / Output Contract

```
classifyBlur(score) -> { band: "reject"|"warn"|"accept", score, rejectBelow, acceptAbove, frameId/timestampNs }

Config: { rejectBelow=40, acceptAbove=80 }  (configurable; remote-config-tunable; validated rejectBelow<=acceptAbove)

Boundaries: score<rejectBelow → reject; score>acceptAbove → accept; else warn (40 and 80 are WARN).
Semantics: reject=don't use; warn=borderline/flag; accept=use. Capture flow wires the concrete action.
```

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT hardcode 40/80 as non-configurable constants — they're content/device-sensitive and meant to be tunable (remote config).
- Do NOT leave boundary inclusivity ambiguous — define exactly which band owns 40 and 80 (WARN, per the spec).
- Do NOT recompute the sharpness metric here — consume the score from the blur-detection task.
- Do NOT collapse to a binary decision and drop the WARN band — the three-band buffer is intentional.
- Do NOT accept invalid config (reject > accept) silently — validate, clamp/error, fall back to defaults.
- Do NOT hardcode the capture accept/reject ACTION here beyond defining band semantics — the capture flow acts on the band.
- Do NOT lose the frame association (id/timestamp).

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Score exactly 40 → WARN; score exactly 80 → WARN (per spec inclusivity).
- [ ] Score 0 / very low → REJECT; score very high → ACCEPT.
- [ ] `rejectBelow == acceptAbove` → empty WARN band (binary); allowed, noted.
- [ ] `rejectBelow > acceptAbove` (invalid) → rejected/clamped to defaults with a clear message; no nonsensical band.
- [ ] Runtime threshold update → applies to subsequent classifications; reported thresholds reflect the active values.
- [ ] Missing/null score → no band (or an explicit "unknown"); do not default to ACCEPT (fail safe toward not-accepting).
- [ ] Negative or NaN score → handled safely (treat as REJECT/unknown, not ACCEPT).

## Constraints

- Boundaries MUST match the spec precisely: `<40` reject, `40–80` warn (inclusive), `>80` accept; document inclusivity.
- Both thresholds MUST be configurable (defaults 40/80), validated (`rejectBelow <= acceptAbove`), remote-config-tunable if applicable.
- The metric MUST come from the blur-detection task; this task only classifies.
- The WARN band MUST be preserved (no silent collapse to binary).
- Band semantics MUST be defined; the concrete capture action stays in the capture flow.
- Invalid config MUST fail safe (defaults), and missing/NaN scores MUST NOT classify as ACCEPT.
- The result MUST carry the frame association and the active thresholds.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Scores below 40 classify REJECT, above 80 ACCEPT, and 40–80 (inclusive) WARN; exactly 40 and exactly 80 are WARN.
- [ ] Thresholds are configurable with defaults 40/80 and validated; invalid config falls back to defaults safely.
- [ ] Remote/runtime threshold updates apply to subsequent classifications; the result reports the active thresholds.
- [ ] The WARN band exists and is used (no binary collapse).
- [ ] The classification consumes the blur-detection score (no metric recomputation) and carries the frame id/timestamp.
- [ ] Band semantics are defined and consumed by the capture flow (reject/warn/accept actions wired there).
- [ ] Missing/NaN/negative scores do not classify as ACCEPT (fail safe).
- [ ] Builds clean; any Flutter wrapper passes `flutter analyze`.

## Testing Instructions

1. Classify scores 0, 39.9, 40, 60, 80, 80.1, 200 → confirm REJECT, REJECT, WARN, WARN, WARN, ACCEPT, ACCEPT respectively (boundary precision).
2. Set custom thresholds (e.g. 30/70) → confirm classification shifts accordingly; set invalid (reject 90, accept 50) → confirm safe fallback to defaults with a clear message.
3. Update thresholds at runtime → confirm subsequent classifications use the new values and the result reports them.
4. Set reject == accept → confirm WARN band is empty (binary behavior), no crash.
5. Pass a NaN/negative/missing score → confirm it does NOT classify ACCEPT (fail safe).
6. Confirm the band carries the correct frame id/timestamp and that the capture flow can act on it.
7. Build + `flutter analyze` → confirm clean.

## Assumptions

- **Builds on blur detection:** Assumes the blur-detection task provides the variance-of-Laplacian score (at 640px width) with a frame id/timestamp. This task classifies that score; it does not compute it. Blocked on that task if absent.
- **Thresholds source:** Defaults 40/80; configurable and likely tunable via P1 remote-config "thresholds" (alongside the stability gate's thresholds). Confirm whether remotely tunable.
- **Boundary inclusivity:** Per the spec, 40 and 80 fall in WARN (`<40` reject, `>80` accept). Confirm if the product wants different inclusivity.
- **Band actions:** This task defines band semantics; the capture/auto-capture flow wires concrete actions (skip/keep/prompt). If the product wants the action defined here, extend accordingly — but default keeps policy meaning here and behavior in the capture flow.
- **Score scale:** Assumes the score is at the blur task's fixed 640px width (so 40/80 are meaningful); changing the downscale width would change the meaningful thresholds.
- **Scope:** Classification policy + config only. Metric computation is the blur-detection task; capture behavior is the capture flow; no sensor/reconstruction work.
- **Stack:** Kotlin; consumes the blur-detection score; config (possibly remote). Adjust to the app's structure.

Generate a coding prompt for this task: Exposure check: mean luminance <40 dark warn, >220 bright warn — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: Android Native (Kotlin)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------->>>>

# NEW: Exposure Check — Mean Luminance (<40 dark warn, >220 bright warn)
# Product: MayasabhaXR — maya-viewer (Flutter client, Android native)
# Scope: New Feature / Native Android (Kotlin) — image-quality processing
# Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline
# Track: Android Native (Kotlin)
# Priority: Medium

---

## Task Description

Compute per-frame mean luminance and warn when the frame is too dark or too bright: mean luminance < 40 → DARK (warn), > 220 → BRIGHT (warn), 40–220 → OK. This is a frame-quality signal parallel to the blur check; it warns (it does NOT reject) so the capture flow / UI can prompt "too dark" / "too bright" while the user adjusts. Thresholds are on the 0–255 luma scale and configurable (likely the P1 remote-config "thresholds").

Two notes that shape the implementation:
- **The Y (luma) plane IS luminance.** For CameraX `ImageAnalysis` (YUV_420_888), average the Y plane directly — respecting `rowStride`/`pixelStride` (the plane is padded; reading it as a contiguous `width×height` buffer is wrong). No YUV→RGB conversion.
- **Mean luminance is scale-independent.** Averaging doesn't change with resolution, so the 40/220 thresholds are valid regardless of downscale — and this can SHARE the downscaled grayscale (and ideally a single frame pass) with the blur-detection task instead of a second traversal.

This provides the exposure signal + band; it does not change camera exposure settings (the focus/exposure-lock task does that) and does not reject frames.

- [ ] Compute mean luminance from the Y plane (stride-correct), reusing the blur task's downscaled grayscale where possible
- [ ] Classify: `<40` DARK, `>220` BRIGHT, else OK — with precise boundary semantics
- [ ] Make both thresholds configurable (defaults 40 / 220), validated; remote-config-tunable if applicable
- [ ] Emit the band + mean luminance associated with the frame (id/timestamp)
- [ ] Run off-main; under real-time use, keep-only-latest and always close the `ImageProxy`
- [ ] Share a single frame pass with the blur check where feasible (efficiency)

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. The blur-detection task — it already extracts/downscales the Y (luma) plane; reuse that buffer / combine into one pass; share the frame id/timestamp association
2. The blur-threshold policy task — parallel banding/config pattern (reject/warn/accept there; here dark/ok/bright warn)
3. P1 `GET /remote-config` ("thresholds") — likely the source for 40/220 (and the blur/stability thresholds)
4. The capture/auto-capture flow + UI — how it surfaces "too dark"/"too bright" hints; whether exposure ever gates (spec says warn only)
5. The focus/exposure-lock task — distinct concern (this measures exposure; that controls it); don't conflate
6. Existing image utilities

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Get luminance correctly
- From the `ImageProxy` (YUV_420_888), read plane[0] (Y) respecting `rowStride`/`pixelStride`. The 8-bit Y values are luminance directly; no color conversion.
- Prefer reusing the blur task's downscaled (640px) grayscale buffer / single pass; if computing independently, subsampling (a grid of pixels) is acceptable since the mean is robust to subsampling.

### Step 2: Mean luminance
- Compute the arithmetic mean of the luma values over the (downscaled/subsampled) frame → a value in [0, 255]. This is scale-independent; downscaling/subsampling does not bias the mean meaningfully.

### Step 3: Classify
```kotlin
enum class ExposureBand { DARK, OK, BRIGHT }
fun classify(mean: Double, darkBelow: Double, brightAbove: Double): ExposureBand =
    when {
        mean < darkBelow   -> ExposureBand.DARK    // < 40
        mean > brightAbove -> ExposureBand.BRIGHT  // > 220
        else               -> ExposureBand.OK      // 40 <= mean <= 220
    }
```
Boundary semantics (precise, per spec): `< darkBelow` → DARK; `> brightAbove` → BRIGHT; otherwise OK. So exactly 40 → OK and exactly 220 → OK. Document inclusivity. Both DARK and BRIGHT are WARN states (not reject).

### Step 4: Configurable, validated thresholds
- Expose `darkBelow` (default 40) and `brightAbove` (default 220) as config; validate `darkBelow < brightAbove`. Invalid config → fall back to defaults with a clear message. Source from config / remote config; runtime updates apply to subsequent frames; report the active thresholds with the result.

### Step 5: Emit + associate
- Emit `{ band: dark|ok|bright, meanLuminance, darkBelow, brightAbove, frameId/timestampNs }`. Record in the sidecar (capture path) and/or stream for a real-time UI hint. Both DARK and BRIGHT surface a warning ("too dark"/"too bright"); OK is good exposure.

### Step 6: Performance + backpressure
- Off the main thread (analyzer executor / I/O). For real-time `ImageAnalysis`, `STRATEGY_KEEP_ONLY_LATEST`; always `close()` the `ImageProxy` (a missed close stalls the analyzer). Combine with the blur pass to avoid two traversals; minimal allocation at rate.

## API / Output Contract

```
computeExposure(frame) -> { band: "dark"|"ok"|"bright", meanLuminance: double, darkBelow, brightAbove, frameId/timestampNs }

Config: { darkBelow=40, brightAbove=220 }  (0–255 scale; configurable; remote-config-tunable; validated darkBelow<brightAbove)

Boundaries: mean<darkBelow → dark; mean>brightAbove → bright; else ok (40 and 220 are OK).
Both dark and bright are WARN (not reject). Y-plane mean (stride-correct); shareable single pass with blur.
```

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT convert YUV→RGB to get luminance — average the Y plane directly.
- Do NOT read the Y plane ignoring `rowStride`/`pixelStride` — padding corrupts the mean.
- Do NOT run a second full frame pass if the blur task already downscales the luma — share the pass/buffer.
- Do NOT treat the extremes as REJECT — they are WARN (the spec gates nothing on exposure).
- Do NOT hardcode 40/220 as non-configurable — they're tunable (remote config), on the 0–255 scale.
- Do NOT run on the main thread; do NOT forget to close the `ImageProxy`; use keep-only-latest.
- Do NOT change camera exposure settings here — that's the focus/exposure-lock task; this only measures.
- Do NOT lose the frame association (id/timestamp).

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] All-black frame (mean ≈ 0) → DARK; all-white (mean ≈ 255) → BRIGHT.
- [ ] Boundary values: exactly 40 → OK; exactly 220 → OK (per spec).
- [ ] High-contrast scene averaging to mid (bright + dark regions) → may classify OK while clipping occurs — a known limitation of MEAN luminance; documented, not papered over (histogram/clipping analysis is a possible future refinement, out of scope).
- [ ] YUV Y plane with `rowStride > width` → read by stride; correct mean.
- [ ] Invalid config (`darkBelow >= brightAbove`) → safe fallback to defaults with a message.
- [ ] Runtime threshold update → applies to subsequent frames; reported thresholds reflect active values.
- [ ] Processing slower than frame rate → keep-only-latest drops frames; `ImageProxy` always closed.
- [ ] NaN/empty frame → no band (or explicit unknown); do not silently classify OK.

## Constraints

- Luminance MUST be the mean of the Y (luma) plane, read stride-correctly; no YUV→RGB conversion.
- Boundaries MUST match the spec: `<40` dark, `>220` bright, `40–220` OK (inclusive); both extremes are WARN (not reject).
- Thresholds MUST be configurable (defaults 40/220, 0–255 scale), validated (`darkBelow < brightAbove`), remote-config-tunable if applicable.
- SHOULD share the downscaled luma / a single frame pass with the blur check (no duplicate traversal).
- Processing MUST be off-main; real-time MUST use keep-only-latest and always close the `ImageProxy`.
- The result MUST carry the frame association and the active thresholds.
- MUST stay scoped to measuring exposure — no camera exposure control, no reject, no reconstruction.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Mean luminance is computed from the Y plane (stride-correct), matching expected values on dark/mid/bright test frames.
- [ ] Classification: `<40` DARK, `>220` BRIGHT, `40–220` OK; exactly 40 and 220 are OK; both extremes reported as WARN.
- [ ] Thresholds are configurable (defaults 40/220) and validated; invalid config falls back safely; runtime updates apply and are reported.
- [ ] The exposure check shares the downscaled luma / single pass with the blur check (no second full traversal), where feasible.
- [ ] The result carries the correct frame id/timestamp; recordable in the sidecar / streamable for UI.
- [ ] Real-time use drops frames under load (keep-latest); `ImageProxy` always closed; off-main; no jank.
- [ ] The mean-luminance limitation (contrast/clipping) is documented; no false precision claimed.
- [ ] No camera exposure setting is changed here; no frame is rejected on exposure.
- [ ] Builds clean; any Flutter wrapper passes `flutter analyze`.

## Testing Instructions

1. Feed an all-dark, a mid-gray, and an all-bright test frame → confirm DARK, OK, BRIGHT respectively, with plausible mean values.
2. Test boundary means 39.9/40/220/220.1 → confirm DARK, OK, OK, BRIGHT (boundary precision).
3. On a device with `rowStride > width` → confirm the mean is correct (stride handling), not skewed by padding.
4. Confirm the exposure mean is computed in the same pass/buffer as the blur metric (no duplicate traversal) where the blur task runs.
5. Set custom thresholds (e.g. 50/200) and an invalid pair (dark 230, bright 50) → confirm shift, then safe fallback with a message.
6. High-contrast scene → confirm it can read OK despite clipping (documented mean limitation).
7. Real-time: slow the analyzer → confirm keep-latest drops frames and `ImageProxy` is always closed (no stall).
8. Confirm frame association + active thresholds in the result.
9. Build + `flutter analyze` → confirm clean.

## Assumptions

- **Shares the blur pipeline:** Assumes the blur-detection task extracts/downscales the Y plane; this reuses that buffer / a combined pass. If independent, subsampling the Y plane is acceptable (mean is robust). Coordinate to avoid two traversals per frame.
- **Thresholds source:** Defaults 40/220 on the 0–255 scale; configurable, likely tunable via P1 remote-config "thresholds" (with blur/stability thresholds). Confirm remote tunability.
- **Boundary inclusivity:** Per spec, 40 and 220 are OK (`<40` dark, `>220` bright). Confirm if different inclusivity is wanted.
- **Mean vs histogram:** Mean luminance is the specified (cheap) metric; it can misjudge high-contrast/clipped scenes. Documented as a known limitation; histogram/clipping analysis is a possible future task, out of scope here.
- **Warn-only:** Per spec, exposure produces warnings only (no reject/gate). If the capture flow should gate on extreme exposure, that's the capture flow's policy, not this task.
- **Distinct from exposure control:** This MEASURES exposure; the focus/exposure-lock task CONTROLS it. Keep separate.
- **Scope:** Exposure measurement + banding only. No camera control, no reject, no sensor/reconstruction work.
- **Stack:** Kotlin + CameraX `ImageAnalysis` Y plane + config (possibly remote). Adjust to the app's structure.

Generate a coding prompt for this task: Local project folder management: /recapture/{projectId}/{jobId}/images/{level}/ — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: Android Native (Kotlin)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------->>>>


# NEW: Local Project Folder Management — /recapture/{projectId}/{jobId}/images/{level}/
# Product: MayasabhaXR — maya-viewer (Flutter client, Android native)
# Scope: New Feature / Native Android (Kotlin) — capture storage hierarchy
# Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline
# Track: Android Native (Kotlin)
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Provide a filesystem manager for the capture storage hierarchy `/recapture/{projectId}/{jobId}/images/{level}/`: resolve the base location, create the nested structure on demand, allocate collision-free paths for frames (+ their sidecars), list/enumerate, account for size/space, sanitize id segments, and clean up jobs/projects/incomplete data. This is the storage backbone the burst/capture task writes into and the EXIF/sidecar task pairs files within.

Key decision (reconciles with P2 permissions): write to **app-scoped storage** (`getExternalFilesDir`/`filesDir`), which requires **NO storage permission**. Capture output is therefore decoupled from `READ/WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` (those permissions, if used at all, are for importing user media, not for capture output). The `/recapture/...` tree is rooted under the app-scoped base, not at filesystem root or shared storage.

- [ ] Resolve an app-scoped base and root `/recapture/{projectId}/{jobId}/images/{level}/` under it
- [ ] Create the nested structure on demand (idempotent, concurrency-safe)
- [ ] Allocate collision-free file paths for frames + sidecars within a level (safe under concurrent burst writes)
- [ ] Sanitize/validate `projectId`/`jobId`/`level` segments (NO path traversal)
- [ ] List/enumerate frames per level/job/project; account for size and check free space
- [ ] Detect and clean up incomplete jobs (app killed mid-capture) and delete jobs/projects on request
- [ ] All filesystem I/O off the main thread

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. The burst/capture task — it writes frames; this manager supplies the destination paths and naming; coordinate the write/allocate handoff
2. The EXIF/sidecar task — sidecars live alongside frames in the same level dir; keep the frame↔sidecar pairing/naming consistent
3. The P2 Android storage-permission task — confirm capture uses app-scoped storage (no permission); the storage permission is for other purposes (media import). Reconcile so capture isn't gated on storage permission.
4. P1 Projects Hub — `projectId` origin (a project owns capture jobs); a delete-project flow may need to orphan/clean capture data
5. App storage conventions — `getExternalFilesDir` vs `filesDir`; where large capture data should live
6. Existing file/IO utilities

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Resolve the base + structure
- Base = an app-scoped dir (e.g. `context.getExternalFilesDir(null)` for larger capture data, or `filesDir`), then `/recapture`. No storage permission required.
- Build paths: `<base>/recapture/<projectId>/<jobId>/images/<level>/`. Provide a function that resolves (and creates if needed) the level dir for a given `(projectId, jobId, level)`.

### Step 2: Sanitize id segments (security)
- `projectId`, `jobId`, `level` come from outside (IDs, capture config). SANITIZE each path segment: reject or strip path separators, `..`, null bytes, and disallowed characters so a crafted id cannot traverse out of the base (`../../etc`). Validate against an allowlist (e.g. alphanumerics, `-`, `_`) or canonicalize and assert the resolved path stays under the base. Reject invalid segments with a clear error.

### Step 3: Idempotent, concurrency-safe creation
- `mkdirs` is idempotent; ensure concurrent calls to create the same level dir don't race/fail. Allocate per-frame file paths so concurrent burst writes never collide: use a unique naming scheme (e.g. `<sequence>_<frameId>.jpg` or a timestamp-based id) and make path allocation thread-safe (atomic counter / per-job synchronization). The sidecar mirrors the frame name (`<frame>.json`).

### Step 4: Enumerate + account
- Provide listing: frames in a level, levels in a job, jobs in a project, projects under recapture. Provide counts and total bytes per level/job/project (for UI/upload/processing).
- Provide a free-space check (available bytes on the storage volume) so the capture flow can verify space before/during a burst and stop gracefully before filling storage (ties to the burst task's storage-full handling).

### Step 5: Incomplete-job detection + cleanup
- A job may be interrupted (app killed mid-burst), leaving a partial level dir. Provide a way to detect incomplete jobs — e.g. a per-job manifest/marker written at job start (and finalized at completion), or a heuristic (no completion marker). Expose: list incomplete jobs, and resume or clean them.
- Provide deletion: delete a level, a job (all its levels/frames/sidecars/manifest), or a project (all jobs). Deletes must be safe (don't delete in-use/active job without guard) and complete (no orphaned files). Hook for P1 project deletion → clean the project's capture data.

### Step 6: Threading + safety
- All filesystem operations (create, list, size, delete) run off the main thread. Handle I/O errors (no space, permission-denied on the volume, missing dir) gracefully with clear results, not crashes.

## API / Manager Contract

```
CaptureStorage (app-scoped; rooted at <appBase>/recapture)
  levelDir(projectId, jobId, level)            -> File (created, sanitized)
  newFramePath(projectId, jobId, level, frameId) -> { framePath, sidecarPath }  // collision-free, concurrency-safe
  listFrames(projectId, jobId, level)          -> [paths]
  counts/sizes(projectId[, jobId[, level]])    -> { count, bytes }
  freeSpaceBytes()                             -> long
  markJobStart/markJobComplete(projectId, jobId)  // for incomplete detection
  listIncompleteJobs()                         -> [jobs]
  deleteLevel/deleteJob/deleteProject(...)     -> result

Guarantees: app-scoped (no storage permission); segments sanitized (no traversal); concurrent-write-safe
naming; off-main I/O; incomplete jobs detectable + cleanable.
```

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT require or use `READ/WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` for capture output — use app-scoped storage (no permission). Do not couple capture to the storage permission.
- Do NOT root `/recapture` at filesystem root or shared/public storage — root it under the app-scoped base.
- Do NOT use `projectId`/`jobId`/`level` unsanitized in paths — prevent path traversal (`..`, separators, null bytes).
- Do NOT assume single-threaded writes — burst is concurrent; path allocation + dir creation must be race-safe and collision-free.
- Do NOT leave partial/orphaned data undetectable — provide incomplete-job detection + cleanup.
- Do NOT run filesystem I/O on the main thread.
- Do NOT delete an active/in-use job without a guard; deletes must be complete (no orphans).
- Do NOT break the frame↔sidecar pairing/naming the EXIF task relies on.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Level dir already exists → idempotent (no error).
- [ ] Concurrent creation of the same level dir / concurrent frame writes → no race, no filename collision.
- [ ] Crafted id with `../` or separators/null bytes → rejected/sanitized; resolved path stays under base.
- [ ] Empty/invalid `projectId`/`jobId`/`level` → rejected with a clear error.
- [ ] Storage full → free-space check fails before/during write; graceful stop, no crash, partial job marked incomplete.
- [ ] App killed mid-burst → partial level dir detectable as an incomplete job (manifest/marker); resumable or cleanable.
- [ ] Very large job (thousands of frames) → listing/size operations are efficient (avoid loading all into memory needlessly).
- [ ] Delete a job/project while a capture is active → guarded; not deleted out from under an active write.
- [ ] P1 project deleted → its capture data cleanable via the hook (no orphaned recapture tree).
- [ ] `level` as int vs string → consistent canonical segment formatting.

## Constraints

- Capture storage MUST be app-scoped (no storage permission); `/recapture` rooted under the app base.
- All path segments MUST be sanitized/validated; resolved paths MUST stay under the base (no traversal).
- Directory creation MUST be idempotent and concurrency-safe; frame/sidecar path allocation MUST be collision-free under concurrent burst writes.
- The manager MUST support enumeration, size/count accounting, and a free-space check.
- Incomplete jobs MUST be detectable (manifest/marker) and cleanable/resumable; deletes MUST be complete and guarded against active jobs.
- All filesystem I/O MUST be off the main thread and error-handled (no crash on full/denied/missing).
- The frame↔sidecar naming/pairing MUST stay consistent with the EXIF/sidecar task.
- MUST stay scoped to storage management — no capture, no metadata content, no processing.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] `/recapture/{projectId}/{jobId}/images/{level}/` is created under an app-scoped base with no storage permission.
- [ ] Crafted ids with `..`/separators/null bytes cannot escape the base (sanitized/rejected); valid ids work.
- [ ] Concurrent burst writes into a level produce unique, non-colliding frame + sidecar files; dir creation doesn't race.
- [ ] Listing, counts, and total bytes are correct per level/job/project; free-space check returns accurate available bytes.
- [ ] An interrupted job is detectable as incomplete (manifest/marker) and can be resumed or cleaned.
- [ ] Deleting a level/job/project removes all associated files (no orphans) and is guarded against active jobs.
- [ ] Storage-full is handled gracefully (checked, no crash, job marked incomplete).
- [ ] Filesystem I/O runs off the main thread; errors (full/denied/missing) return clear results, not crashes.
- [ ] The frame↔sidecar pairing/naming matches the EXIF task's expectations.
- [ ] Builds clean; any Flutter wrapper passes `flutter analyze`.

## Testing Instructions

1. Create a level dir for a `(projectId, jobId, level)` → confirm the app-scoped path exists with no permission prompt.
2. Pass a malicious id (`../../evil`, with separators/null bytes) → confirm rejection/sanitization and that no path escapes the base.
3. Simulate a concurrent burst (many parallel `newFramePath` + writes into one level) → confirm unique non-colliding files and no dir-creation race.
4. List/count/size a populated job → confirm accuracy; check free space → confirm a plausible available-bytes value.
5. Start a job, write some frames, kill the process (no completion marker) → confirm it's detected as incomplete and is resumable/cleanable.
6. Delete a level/job/project → confirm complete removal (no orphans); attempt to delete an active job → confirm it's guarded.
7. Fill storage (or simulate) → confirm the free-space check stops capture gracefully and marks the job incomplete; no crash.
8. Confirm sidecars sit alongside frames with the matching name the EXIF task expects.
9. Profile listing on a large job → confirm efficient enumeration; confirm off-main I/O.
10. Build + `flutter analyze` → confirm clean.

## Assumptions

- **App-scoped storage:** Assumes capture output goes to app-scoped storage (no storage permission). This deliberately reconciles with the P2 storage-permission task — capture is NOT gated on `READ/WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`; that permission (if used) is for importing user media. Confirm the base dir choice (`getExternalFilesDir` for larger data vs `filesDir`).
- **`level` meaning:** Assumes `{level}` is a capture tier/ring/detail-level segment (object capture often uses multiple levels). Treated as a sanitized canonical segment; confirm its exact semantics and whether it's int or string.
- **projectId source:** Assumes `projectId` comes from the P1 Projects Hub (a project owns capture jobs); `jobId` is a capture-session id. A P1 project deletion should clean the corresponding capture data (hook provided).
- **Incomplete detection:** Assumes a per-job manifest/marker (written at start, finalized at completion) to distinguish complete from interrupted jobs; confirm whether a manifest already exists (the EXIF task writes per-frame sidecars; a job-level manifest may be added here).
- **Concurrency:** Assumes the burst task writes frames concurrently; this manager guarantees collision-free, race-safe path allocation and creation.
- **Scope:** Storage hierarchy management only — it does not capture frames (burst task), write metadata content (EXIF task), or process/upload (later tasks). It provides paths, structure, accounting, and cleanup.
- **Stack:** Kotlin + app-scoped File storage + off-main I/O. Adjust to the app's structure.

Generate a coding prompt for this task: Storage cleanup on project delete — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: Android Native (Kotlin)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------->>>>


# NEW: Storage Cleanup on Project Delete (local capture data)
# Product: MayasabhaXR — maya-viewer (Flutter client, Android native)
# Scope: New Feature / Native Android (Kotlin) — capture storage cleanup
# Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline
# Track: Android Native (Kotlin)
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

When a project is deleted, purge its local capture data — the entire `/recapture/{projectId}/` tree (all jobs, levels, frames, sidecars, manifests) under app-scoped storage — to reclaim space. This is the cleanup hook the storage-manager task reserved; it deletes LOCAL capture data only (the server-side project record is P1's concern).

This surfaces a cross-phase decision that MUST be reconciled (see Reconciliation): P1's project delete is a SOFT delete (recoverable). If this purges local capture images on that soft delete, a subsequent "restore" can recover the project record but NOT its (large, local) capture images. Decide: purge-on-soft-delete (reclaim space, accept that local capture isn't restorable), purge only on hard delete / after a grace period, or retain until explicit purge.

- [ ] Provide a hook the project-delete flow calls to purge a project's local capture data by `projectId`
- [ ] Delete the whole `/recapture/{projectId}/` tree completely (no orphans), via the storage manager
- [ ] Reconcile with P1 soft-delete semantics (purge timing — see Reconciliation; do not silently pick)
- [ ] Guard against deleting data for an ACTIVE capture job; sanitize/exact-match `projectId` (no traversal/wrong-tree)
- [ ] Be idempotent (already-gone project → no-op success) and complete (handle locked/partial-failure)
- [ ] Report reclaimed space + success/failure; run off the main thread
- [ ] (Optional) sweep orphaned capture data for projects that no longer exist

## Files to Inspect First

> Adjust these paths to the real repo layout. They reflect the assumed conventions in the Assumptions section — confirm before the agent edits anything.

1. The local capture storage-manager task — its `deleteProject` and the `/recapture/{projectId}/...` structure; this task drives that deletion and the active-job guard
2. P1 `DELETE /projects/:id` (SOFT delete) + the client delete flow — WHERE/WHEN to trigger local purge; reconcile soft-delete vs purge timing
3. The burst/capture task — how to detect an ACTIVE capture job for the project (guard before deleting)
4. The Hive permission/flow or any local project cache — the known-project list (for the optional orphan sweep)
5. App storage conventions — the app-scoped base the recapture tree is rooted under
6. Existing `MethodChannel` conventions — the purge hook from the delete flow

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Provide the purge hook
- Expose `purgeProjectCaptureData(projectId)` over a `MethodChannel`, called by the client's project-delete flow. Sanitize/validate `projectId` (exact match, no separators/`..`/null bytes — no traversal, no wrong-tree deletion). Resolve `/recapture/{projectId}/` under the app-scoped base via the storage manager.

### Step 2: Reconcile purge timing (decision required)
- Reconcile with P1 soft-delete:
  - **Option A (default): purge on delete.** The user deletes → reclaim space immediately. Document that a restored project will NOT have its local capture images (server record restorable, local capture not).
  - **Option B: purge on hard delete / after a grace period.** Retain local capture while the project is soft-deleted/recoverable; purge on hard delete or after N days. Requires knowing the hard-delete/grace signal.
  - **Option C: retain until explicit purge.** Keep capture data; offer a manual "free up space" action.
- Implement the CONFIRMED option; do not silently choose. Default A unless the product wants restore to recover local capture.

### Step 3: Active-job guard
- Before deleting, check whether a capture job for this `projectId` is ACTIVE/in-progress. If so, do NOT delete out from under an active write — either refuse with a clear error, or cancel/stop the active capture first per the confirmed policy. Never corrupt an in-flight job.

### Step 4: Complete, idempotent deletion
- Delete the entire project subtree recursively via the storage manager (all jobs/levels/frames/sidecars/manifests). 
- Idempotent: if `/recapture/{projectId}/` doesn't exist (already purged / never captured), return no-op success.
- Completeness: ensure no orphaned files/dirs remain. If some files can't be deleted (locked/in-use), report which and the partial result; offer retry rather than leaving silent partial state.

### Step 5: Report + threading
- Compute and report reclaimed bytes (size before delete) and a success/partial/failure result back to Flutter.
- Run all I/O off the main thread (large deletes can be slow); for very large trees, do not block; report progress/completion.

### Step 6: (Optional) orphan sweep
- Provide an optional sweep: enumerate `/recapture/*` project dirs, compare against the known-project list (local cache / server), and purge capture data for projects that no longer exist (e.g. deleted while the app was off). Apply the SAME guards (active job, sanitization) and the same purge-timing policy. Useful to reclaim space from data that missed its delete hook.

## API / Contract

```
MethodChannel: "mayasabhaxr/capture_storage" (or app convention)
  purgeProjectCaptureData(projectId) -> { reclaimedBytes, status: "ok"|"partial"|"refused"|"noop", failed?: [paths] }
  sweepOrphanedCaptureData()         -> { purgedProjects: [...], reclaimedBytes }   // optional

Rules: app-scoped /recapture/{projectId} only; projectId sanitized (no traversal); active-job guarded;
idempotent + complete; purge timing reconciled with P1 soft-delete; off-main; reclaimed space reported.
```

## What NOT to Do

- Do NOT purge local capture data on soft-delete WITHOUT reconciling the restore implication — a restored project won't have its local images. Reconcile/confirm the policy.
- Do NOT delete data for an active/in-progress capture job — guard (refuse or cancel-first per policy).
- Do NOT use an unsanitized `projectId` in the path — prevent traversal and wrong-tree deletion; exact match only.
- Do NOT leave orphaned/partial data silently — deletion must be complete or report what failed.
- Do NOT crash if the project dir doesn't exist — idempotent no-op.
- Do NOT block the main thread on large deletes.
- Do NOT perform the SERVER-side project delete here — that's P1; this is local capture cleanup only.
- Do NOT delete outside `/recapture/{projectId}/` (never the whole recapture tree or other projects).

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Project dir doesn't exist (never captured / already purged) → no-op success.
- [ ] Active capture job for the project → guarded (refuse or cancel-first); no corruption of an in-flight job.
- [ ] Some files locked/in-use → partial result reported with the failed paths; retry offered; no silent partial.
- [ ] Very large project (many GB / thousands of files) → off-main, doesn't block; progress/completion reported.
- [ ] Crafted/invalid `projectId` → sanitized/rejected; cannot traverse or hit another project's tree.
- [ ] Concurrent purge requests for the same project → safe (idempotent; one effective delete).
- [ ] Soft-delete then restore (Option A) → documented that local capture is gone; restore recovers only the record.
- [ ] Orphan sweep finds data for an unknown project → purged under the same guards/policy.
- [ ] Storage manager unavailable / I/O error → graceful failure result, no crash.

## Constraints

- Purge MUST target only `/recapture/{projectId}/` under the app-scoped base; `projectId` sanitized/exact-match; never delete other projects or the whole tree.
- Purge timing MUST be reconciled with P1 soft-delete (confirmed option), not silently chosen.
- An active capture job MUST be guarded (refuse or cancel-first); never delete an in-flight job's data.
- Deletion MUST be idempotent (missing → no-op) and complete (no orphans) or report partial failure with paths.
- Reclaimed space + status MUST be reported; all I/O off the main thread.
- This task MUST NOT do the server-side project delete (P1) — local capture cleanup only.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] `purgeProjectCaptureData(projectId)` removes the entire `/recapture/{projectId}/` tree and reports reclaimed bytes.
- [ ] An invalid/crafted `projectId` cannot traverse out or delete another project's tree (sanitized/exact-match).
- [ ] Purging a never-captured / already-purged project is a no-op success (idempotent).
- [ ] An active capture job for the project is guarded — purge refuses or cancels-first per policy; no in-flight corruption.
- [ ] Locked/undeletable files produce a partial result listing failed paths (no silent partial); retry works.
- [ ] Large-tree deletion runs off-main without blocking; progress/completion reported.
- [ ] The purge-timing policy matches the confirmed P1 soft-delete reconciliation and is documented (restore implication clear under Option A).
- [ ] (If implemented) the orphan sweep purges capture data for unknown projects under the same guards.
- [ ] Only `/recapture/{projectId}/` is ever deleted; never other projects or outside the tree.
- [ ] Builds clean; the Flutter wrapper passes `flutter analyze`.

## Testing Instructions

1. Capture data for a project, then `purgeProjectCaptureData(projectId)` → confirm the whole tree is gone and reclaimed bytes are reported.
2. Pass a crafted `projectId` (`../otherProject`, separators, null) → confirm rejection and that nothing outside `/recapture/{projectId}/` is touched.
3. Purge a project with no capture data → confirm no-op success.
4. Start an active capture for a project, then request purge → confirm the guard (refuse or cancel-first); confirm no corruption of the active job.
5. Lock a file in the tree (simulate in-use) → confirm a partial result with the failed path and that retry completes.
6. Create a multi-GB tree → confirm off-main deletion, no main-thread block, progress/completion reported.
7. (Option A) purge then attempt restore → confirm the documented behavior (record restorable, local capture gone).
8. (If implemented) leave orphaned data for a deleted project → run the sweep → confirm it's purged under the guards.
9. Confirm no other project's data is ever affected.
10. Build + `flutter analyze` → confirm clean.

## Assumptions

- **Builds on storage manager:** Assumes the storage-manager task provides `deleteProject`/tree deletion and the active-job signal. This task drives those with guards, reconciliation, and reporting. Blocked on the storage manager if absent.
- **Soft-delete reconciliation (KEY):** P1's project delete is soft (recoverable). Defaulted to Option A (purge on delete; local capture not restorable on restore), but this MUST be confirmed with the product — Options B (purge on hard-delete/grace) and C (retain until explicit) are alternatives. This decision spans P1 and this phase.
- **Trigger:** Assumes the client project-delete flow calls the purge hook. Confirm whether purge fires on the user's delete action, on server-confirmed delete, or on hard-delete, consistent with the chosen option.
- **Active-job policy:** Assumes either refuse-if-active or cancel-then-purge; confirm which.
- **Scope:** Local capture-data cleanup only — NOT the server-side project delete (P1), NOT the project record/cache removal (Hub), NOT processing. Only `/recapture/{projectId}/` under the app-scoped base.
- **Orphan sweep:** Optional; included if the product wants to reclaim space from data that missed its delete hook. Uses the same guards/policy.
- **Stack:** Kotlin + the storage manager + app-scoped File I/O + `MethodChannel`. Adjust to the app's structure.
