   patterns already used; do not introduce a second pattern
4. `lib/channels/blur_channel.dart` — use as the template for
   `camera_channel.dart` (channel name constant placement, return type style)
5. `ios/Runner/Info.plist` — confirm `NSCameraUsageDescription` key exists;
   if absent, add it (value: `"Used to capture photos for 3D scanning"`)

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Create CameraCapturePipeline.swift

Create `ios/Runner/CameraCapturePipeline.swift`. This file has zero Flutter
imports — all Flutter wiring lives in a separate handler file.

```swift
import AVFoundation

final class CameraCapturePipeline: NSObject {

    // Serial queue for all session operations — never touch the session
    // from the main thread or any other queue
    private let sessionQueue = DispatchQueue(label: "com.mayasabhaxr.camera.session")
    private let session      = AVCaptureSession()
    private var photoOutput  = AVCapturePhotoOutput()

    // Pending capture completions — keyed by the unique ID assigned to each
    // AVCapturePhotoSettings request; guards against interleaved captures
    private var pendingCompletions: [Int64: (Result<URL, Error>) -> Void] = [:]
    private let completionLock = NSLock()

    enum CaptureError: Error {
        case noCameraAvailable
        case sessionConfigurationFailed
        case codecUnavailable
        case noPhotoData
        case writeFailed(underlying: Error)
    }

    func start(completion: @escaping (Result<Void, CaptureError>) -> Void) {
        sessionQueue.async { [weak self] in
            guard let self else { return }
            do {
                try self.configureSession()
                self.session.startRunning()
                completion(.success(()))
            } catch let e as CaptureError {
                completion(.failure(e))
            } catch {
                completion(.failure(.sessionConfigurationFailed))
            }
        }
    }

    func stop() {
        sessionQueue.async { [weak self] in
            self?.session.stopRunning()
        }
    }

    func capturePhoto(format: String,
                      completion: @escaping (Result<URL, Error>) -> Void) {
        sessionQueue.async { [weak self] in
            guard let self else { return }

            let useHEIC = format == "heic" &&
                self.photoOutput.availablePhotoCodecTypes.contains(.heic)
            let codec: AVVideoCodecType = useHEIC ? .heic : .jpeg

            let settings = AVCapturePhotoSettings(format: [AVVideoCodecKey: codec])
            settings.isHighResolutionPhotoEnabled =
                self.photoOutput.isHighResolutionCaptureEnabled

            self.completionLock.lock()
            self.pendingCompletions[settings.uniqueID] = completion
            self.completionLock.unlock()

            self.photoOutput.capturePhoto(with: settings, delegate: self)
        }
    }

    // MARK: - Private

    private func configureSession() throws {
        guard let device = AVCaptureDevice.default(
            .builtInWideAngleCamera, for: .video, position: .back
        ) else { throw CaptureError.noCameraAvailable }

        let input = try AVCaptureDeviceInput(device: device)

        session.beginConfiguration()
        session.sessionPreset = .photo

        guard session.canAddInput(input) else {
            session.commitConfiguration()
            throw CaptureError.sessionConfigurationFailed
        }
        session.addInput(input)

        photoOutput.isHighResolutionCaptureEnabled = true
        guard session.canAddOutput(photoOutput) else {
            session.commitConfiguration()
            throw CaptureError.sessionConfigurationFailed
        }
        session.addOutput(photoOutput)
        session.commitConfiguration()
    }
}
```

### Step 2: Implement AVCapturePhotoCaptureDelegate

Extend `CameraCapturePipeline` in the same file with the delegate conformance.
Do NOT create a separate delegate class:

```swift
extension CameraCapturePipeline: AVCapturePhotoCaptureDelegate {

    func photoOutput(_ output: AVCapturePhotoOutput,
                     didFinishProcessingPhoto photo: AVCapturePhoto,
                     error: Error?) {
        completionLock.lock()
        let completion = pendingCompletions.removeValue(forKey: photo.resolvedSettings.uniqueID)
        completionLock.unlock()

        guard let completion else { return }

        if let error {
            completion(.failure(error))
            return
        }

        guard let data = photo.fileDataRepresentation() else {
            completion(.failure(CaptureError.noPhotoData))
            return
        }

        // Determine extension from actual codec used
        let isHEIC = photo.resolvedSettings.availableRawPhotoFileTypes.isEmpty &&
            (photo.resolvedSettings.processedFileType == .heic)
        // Simpler: check metadata codec
        let ext = (data.prefix(12).contains(0x66) /* 'f' in 'ftyp' HEIC box */)
            ? "heic" : "jpg"
        // Use resolved codec approach instead — more reliable:
        let fileExt: String
        if let codec = photo.resolvedSettings.photoCodecType,
           codec == .heic {
            fileExt = "heic"
        } else {
            fileExt = "jpg"
        }

        let fileName = UUID().uuidString + "." + fileExt
        let url = URL(fileURLWithPath: NSTemporaryDirectory())
            .appendingPathComponent(fileName)

        do {
            try data.write(to: url, options: .atomic)
            completion(.success(url))
        } catch {
            completion(.failure(CaptureError.writeFailed(underlying: error)))
        }
    }
}
```

**Note:** Remove the `isHEIC` and `ext` intermediate variables — only
`fileExt` derived from `photo.resolvedSettings.photoCodecType` should remain.
The snippet above shows both approaches for clarity; use only the
`resolvedSettings.photoCodecType` path in the final code.

### Step 3: Create CameraChannelHandler.swift

Create `ios/Runner/CameraChannelHandler.swift`. This file owns only the
Flutter ↔ Swift bridge. It holds a `CameraCapturePipeline` instance and
manages session start/stop in response to Flutter calls.

```swift
import Flutter

final class CameraChannelHandler {

    static let channelName = "com.mayasabhaxr.maya_viewer/camera"
    private let pipeline   = CameraCapturePipeline()
    private var isStarted  = false

    func handle(call: FlutterMethodCall, result: @escaping FlutterResult) {
        switch call.method {
        case "startSession":
            startSession(result: result)
        case "stopSession":
            pipeline.stop()
            isStarted = false
            result(nil)
        case "capturePhoto":
            capturePhoto(call: call, result: result)
        default:
            result(FlutterMethodNotImplemented)
        }
    }

    private func startSession(result: @escaping FlutterResult) {
        guard !isStarted else {
            result(nil)   // idempotent
            return
        }
        pipeline.start { [weak self] outcome in
            DispatchQueue.main.async {
                switch outcome {
                case .success:
                    self?.isStarted = true
                    result(nil)
                case .failure(let error):
                    result(FlutterError(code: "SESSION_ERROR",
                                        message: error.localizedDescription,
                                        details: nil))
                }
            }
        }
    }

    private func capturePhoto(call: FlutterMethodCall,
                               result: @escaping FlutterResult) {
        guard isStarted else {
            result(FlutterError(code: "SESSION_NOT_STARTED",
                                message: "Call startSession before capturePhoto",
                                details: nil))
            return
        }
        let format = (call.arguments as? [String: Any])?["format"] as? String ?? "heic"
        pipeline.capturePhoto(format: format) { outcome in
            DispatchQueue.main.async {
                switch outcome {
                case .success(let url):
                    result(url.path)
                case .failure(let error):
                    result(FlutterError(code: "CAPTURE_FAILED",
                                        message: error.localizedDescription,
                                        details: nil))
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
```

### Step 4: Register the MethodChannel in AppDelegate

In `AppDelegate.swift`, inside `application(_:didFinishLaunchingWithOptions:)`,
after the existing blur and orientation channel registrations:

```swift
let cameraHandler = CameraChannelHandler()
let cameraChannel = FlutterMethodChannel(
    name: CameraChannelHandler.channelName,
    binaryMessenger: controller.binaryMessenger
)
cameraChannel.setMethodCallHandler(cameraHandler.handle)
```

Store `cameraHandler` and `cameraChannel` as strong `AppDelegate` instance
properties — do not declare them as local `let` variables inside the function
body, as ARC will immediately deallocate them.

### Step 5: Verify NSCameraUsageDescription in Info.plist

Open `ios/Runner/Info.plist`. If `NSCameraUsageDescription` is absent, add:

```xml
<key>NSCameraUsageDescription</key>
<string>Used to capture photos for 3D scanning</string>
```

Do not change any other Info.plist entry.

### Step 6: Create lib/channels/camera_channel.dart

```dart
import 'package:flutter/services.dart';

const _channel = MethodChannel('com.mayasabhaxr.maya_viewer/camera');

/// Must be called once before [capturePhoto]. Idempotent.
Future<void> startCameraSession() async {
  await _channel.invokeMethod<void>('startSession');
}

/// Releases the AVCaptureSession. Call when the camera screen is disposed.
Future<void> stopCameraSession() async {
  await _channel.invokeMethod<void>('stopSession');
}

/// Returns the absolute path to the captured temp file.
/// [format] must be `"heic"` or `"jpeg"`. Defaults to `"heic"`.
/// Automatically falls back to JPEG on devices that do not support HEIC.
Future<String> capturePhoto({String format = 'heic'}) async {
  final path = await _channel.invokeMethod<String>(
    'capturePhoto',
    {'format': format},
  );
  if (path == null || path.isEmpty) {
    throw PlatformException(
      code: 'CAPTURE_FAILED',
      message: 'capturePhoto returned a null or empty path',
    );
  }
  return path;
}
```

## API / Data Contract

MethodChannel name: `com.mayasabhaxr.maya_viewer/camera`

**Method: `startSession`**
Arguments: none
Returns: `null` on success, `FlutterError(code: "SESSION_ERROR")` on failure

**Method: `stopSession`**
Arguments: none
Returns: `null` always

**Method: `capturePhoto`**
Arguments (Map, optional):
{

"format": String   // "heic" | "jpeg" — defaults to "heic" if absent

}
Returns on success: `String` — absolute path to temp file, e.g.
`/private/var/mobile/.../tmp/3F2A1B4C-….heic`

Error codes:
- `SESSION_NOT_STARTED` — `capturePhoto` called before `startSession`
- `CAPTURE_FAILED` — `AVCapturePhotoOutput` delegate returned an error or
  `fileDataRepresentation()` returned nil
- `SESSION_ERROR` — session configuration failed (no camera, permission denied)

## Analytics Events

Event: `photo_captured`
Trigger: when `didFinishProcessingPhoto` completes successfully and the file
is written to disk (fire from `CameraChannelHandler.capturePhoto` success path,
before calling `result`)
Properties:
  - `format`: String — `"heic"` or `"jpeg"` (actual codec used, not requested)
  - `file_size_bytes`: Int — byte count of the written file
  - `session_id`: String — UUID generated at `startSession` time, reset on
    `stopSession`; used to group captures in the same session

## What NOT to Change

- Do NOT modify `OrientationStreamHandler.swift` or `BlurMethodChannelHandler.swift`
- Do NOT modify the blur or orientation channel registrations in `AppDelegate.swift`
- Do NOT add `AVCaptureVideoDataOutput` or any video output — this task is
  still-photo only; a video preview layer is a separate future task
- Do NOT save photos to `PHPhotoLibrary` / Camera Roll — write to
  `NSTemporaryDirectory()` only; the Dart layer decides what to do with the file
- Do NOT request `NSPhotoLibraryAddUsageDescription` — it is not needed here
- Do NOT change any existing Dart channel files (`blur_channel.dart`,
  `orientation_channel.dart`)
- Do NOT hold a strong reference to `AVCapturePhoto` beyond the delegate
  callback — it holds the full-resolution pixel buffer and causes significant
  memory pressure if retained

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Camera permission not granted → `AVCaptureDeviceInput` initialiser throws;
  return `FlutterError(code: "SESSION_ERROR", message: "Camera permission denied")`
  — do NOT prompt the user from native code; permission prompting is the Dart
  layer's responsibility
- [ ] HEIC requested but device does not support it (pre-A10 chip) →
  `availablePhotoCodecTypes` will not contain `.heic`; silently capture JPEG
  and return a `.jpg` path — do NOT return an error
- [ ] `capturePhoto` called while a previous capture is still in-flight →
  `pendingCompletions` supports concurrent entries (keyed by `uniqueID`); both
  captures proceed independently; do NOT serialise or queue them
- [ ] Temp directory full → `data.write(to:)` throws; return
  `FlutterError(code: "CAPTURE_FAILED", message: "writeFailed: ...")`
- [ ] `stopSession` called while a capture is in-flight → the capture will
  still complete via the delegate callback; `pendingCompletions` will resolve
  normally; do NOT cancel in-flight captures on stop
- [ ] `startSession` called multiple times without `stopSession` in between →
  `isStarted` guard makes it idempotent; return `null` immediately on the
  second call without reconfiguring the session

## Constraints

- All `AVCaptureSession` operations (`beginConfiguration`, `commitConfiguration`,
  `startRunning`, `stopRunning`, `capturePhoto`) must be called exclusively on
  `sessionQueue` — never on the main queue or any other queue
- All `FlutterResult` callbacks must be dispatched back to `DispatchQueue.main`
  before calling `result(...)` — `FlutterResult` is not thread-safe
- `CameraCapturePipeline` must have zero Flutter imports — it must be
  independently testable without a Flutter engine (same constraint as
  `BlurDetector`)
- `NSTemporaryDirectory()` files are not automatically cleaned up; the Dart
  layer is responsible for deleting the file after use — do NOT add any
  cleanup logic inside the Swift pipeline
- Use `session.sessionPreset = .photo` — do not use `.high` or `.medium`;
  `.photo` is the only preset that enables `isHighResolutionCaptureEnabled`
  on `AVCapturePhotoOutput`
- Do not use the deprecated `AVCapturePhotoOutput.capturePhoto` overload that
  accepts `AVCapturePhotoSettings` with a `format` dictionary of type
  `[String: Any]` containing `AVVideoCodecKey` as a raw string — use the
  `AVVideoCodecType` enum constant

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Calling `startSession` → `capturePhoto` → `stopSession` from Dart on a
  physical iPhone 12 or newer returns a valid `.heic` file path; the file
  exists on disk and opens correctly in iOS Photos app when shared
- [ ] On a device that does not support HEIC (or when `format: "jpeg"` is
  passed), the returned file path has a `.jpg` extension and the file is a
  valid JPEG (verify with `UIImage(contentsOfFile:)` returning non-nil)
- [ ] Calling `capturePhoto` without calling `startSession` first returns
  `FlutterError(code: "SESSION_NOT_STARTED")` and does not crash
- [ ] Calling `startSession` twice in a row without `stopSession` in between
  returns `null` on the second call and does not create a second
  `AVCaptureSession` (verify with Xcode memory graph — only one session object)
- [ ] Denying camera permission in iOS Settings → `startSession` returns
  `FlutterError(code: "SESSION_ERROR")` and does not crash
- [ ] The captured HEIC file is smaller than the equivalent JPEG of the same
  scene by at least 20% (verify with two sequential captures of the same scene,
  one HEIC one JPEG, compare file sizes via Dart `File(path).lengthSync()`)
- [ ] `photo_captured` analytics event fires with correct `format`, non-zero
  `file_size_bytes`, and a consistent `session_id` across multiple captures in
  the same session
- [ ] `flutter build ios --no-codesign` compiles without warnings
- [ ] `dart analyze` reports zero new issues

## Testing Instructions

1. Physical device only — `AVCaptureSession` does not work on Simulator.
   Run on iPhone 12 or newer (HEIC support confirmed on A-series chips).

2. Happy path — HEIC:
   Call `startCameraSession()`, wait 500ms for session to stabilise, call
   `capturePhoto(format: 'heic')`. Log the returned path. Confirm file exists,

   After a successful capture, confirm the `photo_captured` event is logged

8. Build check:
   Run `flutter build ios --no-codesign` and `dart analyze`. Both must pass
   with zero new warnings or errors.

## Assumptions

- Assumed: The project targets iOS 14.0 or later — `AVCapturePhotoOutput` with
  HEIC codec and `photoCodecType` on `AVCaptureResolvedPhotoSettings` requires
  iOS 11+; `isHighResolutionCaptureEnabled` API used here requires iOS 10+.
  If the minimum deployment target is below iOS 11, wrap HEIC codec usage in
  `if #available(iOS 11, *)`.
- Assumed: The app already requests camera permission from the Dart layer before
  calling `startSession` — the native pipeline does not show the system
  permission prompt; it simply fails with `SESSION_ERROR` if permission is absent.
- Assumed: No live camera preview (viewfinder) is required in this task — only
  silent capture. If a preview layer is needed, that is a separate task that
  will add a `startPreview(layerId:)` method to the pipeline.
- Assumed: Analytics infrastructure (`logEvent`) already exists and is callable

  Generate a coding prompt for this task: Background capture session survival on app backgrounding — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: iOS Native (Swift)

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------->>>>>>>>>>>>>>>>

# FEATURE: Background Capture Session Survival on App Backgrounding
# Product: maya-viewer (iOS Native)
# Scope: New Feature
# Priority: Critical

---

## Task Description

Add lifecycle management to `CameraCapturePipeline` so that the
`AVCaptureSession` survives app backgrounding gracefully — pausing on
background entry and resuming on foreground return — without crashing,
leaking, or requiring Dart intervention. iOS terminates apps that hold an
active camera session in the background without the `voip` or
`audio` background mode entitlement; this task adds the correct interruption-
handling so the session suspends safely and restores automatically.

- [ ] Observe `UIApplication.didEnterBackgroundNotification` in
  `CameraCapturePipeline` and call `session.stopRunning()` on `sessionQueue`
- [ ] Observe `UIApplication.willEnterForegroundNotification` and call
  `session.startRunning()` on `sessionQueue` only if the session was running
  before backgrounding (i.e. not if `stopSession()` was explicitly called)
- [ ] Observe `AVCaptureSessionWasInterruptedNotification` and
  `AVCaptureSessionInterruptionEndedNotification` to handle interruptions
  caused by incoming calls, FaceID, or other camera-claiming system events;
  log interruption reason in `#if DEBUG` and resume after interruption ends
- [ ] Notify Flutter of session state changes (suspended / resumed / interrupted
  / error) via a new EventChannel `com.mayasabhaxr.maya_viewer/camera_state`;
  do NOT reuse or modify the existing `com.mayasabhaxr.maya_viewer/camera`
  MethodChannel
- [ ] Create `CameraStateStreamHandler.swift` to own the EventChannel; wire it
  in `AppDelegate.swift` following the existing handler registration pattern
- [ ] Create `lib/channels/camera_state_channel.dart` on the Dart side
- [ ] Unregister all `NotificationCenter` observers inside `CameraCapturePipeline.stop()`
  and in `deinit` to prevent dangling observers

## Files to Inspect First

1. `ios/Runner/CameraCapturePipeline.swift` — this is the primary file being
   modified; understand the full `start()` / `stop()` / `capturePhoto()`
   lifecycle, the `sessionQueue`, and how `isStarted` state is currently
   tracked before adding any observer logic
2. `ios/Runner/CameraChannelHandler.swift` — understand how `isStarted` is
   managed from the Flutter side; the new background-pause logic must NOT
   corrupt the `isStarted` flag that `capturePhoto` depends on
3. `ios/Runner/AppDelegate.swift` — identify where existing channel handlers
   are registered as strong instance properties; add the new
   `CameraStateStreamHandler` in the same block and pattern
4. `ios/Runner/OrientationStreamHandler.swift` — use as the structural
   reference for implementing `FlutterStreamHandler` conformance in
   `CameraStateStreamHandler`
5. `lib/channels/camera_channel.dart` — use as template for
   `camera_state_channel.dart` (channel name constant style, Stream typedef)

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Add session state tracking to CameraCapturePipeline

Add two new private properties directly below the existing `isStarted`-
equivalent state (wherever session running state is currently tracked in
`CameraCapturePipeline.swift`):

```swift
// True only when the session was running immediately before backgrounding.
// Prevents auto-resume if stopSession() was called explicitly by Flutter.
private var wasRunningBeforeBackground = false

// Weak reference sink — set by CameraStateStreamHandler when Dart subscribes
weak var stateSink: FlutterEventSink?   // declared as a closure typealias below
```

Because `FlutterEventSink` is a typealias for `@escaping (Any?) -> Void`, it
cannot be declared `weak`. Instead, wrap it:

```swift
// Box that allows the pipeline to send state events without importing Flutter
var onStateChange: ((String) -> Void)?
```

`CameraStateStreamHandler` will assign a closure to `onStateChange` that
forwards to the Flutter sink. `CameraCapturePipeline` never imports Flutter.

Valid state strings (the full set — do not add others):
- `"running"` — session is active and capturing
- `"suspended"` — paused due to backgrounding; will auto-resume
- `"interrupted"` — system claimed the camera (call, FaceID, etc.)
- `"resumed"` — returned from interrupted state
- `"stopped"` — explicitly stopped by Flutter via `stopSession()`
- `"error"` — session runtime error (`AVCaptureSessionRuntimeErrorNotification`)

### Step 2: Register NotificationCenter observers in start()

At the end of the `configureSession()` private method (after
`session.commitConfiguration()`), register all observers. All observers must
dispatch their work onto `sessionQueue` — never directly execute session
operations in the notification handler closure itself:

```swift
private func registerObservers() {
    let nc = NotificationCenter.default

    nc.addObserver(forName: UIApplication.didEnterBackgroundNotification,
                   object: nil, queue: nil) { [weak self] _ in
        self?.handleDidEnterBackground()
    }
    nc.addObserver(forName: UIApplication.willEnterForegroundNotification,
                   object: nil, queue: nil) { [weak self] _ in
        self?.handleWillEnterForeground()
    }
    nc.addObserver(forName: .AVCaptureSessionWasInterrupted,
                   object: nil, queue: nil) { [weak self] note in
        self?.handleInterruption(notification: note)
    }
    nc.addObserver(forName: .AVCaptureSessionInterruptionEnded,
                   object: nil, queue: nil) { [weak self] _ in
        self?.handleInterruptionEnded()
    }
    nc.addObserver(forName: .AVCaptureSessionRuntimeError,
                   object: nil, queue: nil) { [weak self] note in
        self?.handleRuntimeError(notification: note)
    }
}
```

Call `registerObservers()` at the end of `configureSession()`, before
`session.commitConfiguration()` is reached — so that observers are live as
soon as the session is valid.

### Step 3: Implement background/foreground handlers

```swift
private func handleDidEnterBackground() {
    sessionQueue.async { [weak self] in
        guard let self else { return }
        if self.session.isRunning {
            self.wasRunningBeforeBackground = true
            self.session.stopRunning()
            self.onStateChange?("suspended")
        }
    }
}

private func handleWillEnterForeground() {
    sessionQueue.async { [weak self] in
        guard let self else { return }
        guard self.wasRunningBeforeBackground else { return }
        self.wasRunningBeforeBackground = false
        self.session.startRunning()
        self.onStateChange?("running")
    }
}
```

### Step 4: Implement interruption handlers

```swift
private func handleInterruption(notification: Notification) {
    sessionQueue.async { [weak self] in
        guard let self else { return }
        #if DEBUG
        if let reasonValue = notification.userInfo?[
            AVCaptureSessionInterruptionReasonKey] as? Int,
           let reason = AVCaptureSession.InterruptionReason(rawValue: reasonValue) {
            print("[CameraSession] Interrupted — reason: \(reason)")
        }
        #endif
        self.onStateChange?("interrupted")
    }
}

private func handleInterruptionEnded() {
    sessionQueue.async { [weak self] in
        guard let self else { return }
        // Only restart if the session was in a running state before interruption
        // (i.e. not explicitly stopped by Flutter)
        if !self.session.isRunning && self.wasRunningBeforeBackground == false {
            self.session.startRunning()
        }
        self.onStateChange?("resumed")
    }
}
```

### Step 5: Implement runtime error handler

A runtime error (e.g. camera hardware reset) may allow recovery by restarting:

```swift
private func handleRuntimeError(notification: Notification) {
    sessionQueue.async { [weak self] in
        guard let self else { return }
        let error = notification.userInfo?[AVCaptureSessionErrorKey] as? AVError
        #if DEBUG
        print("[CameraSession] Runtime error: \(String(describing: error))")
        #endif
        // Attempt a single automatic restart for media services reset errors
        if error?.code == .mediaServicesWereReset {
            self.session.startRunning()
            self.onStateChange?("running")
        } else {
            self.onStateChange?("error")
        }
    }
}
```

### Step 6: Remove observers in stop() and deinit

In the existing `stop()` method of `CameraCapturePipeline`, after
`session.stopRunning()`, add:

```swift
NotificationCenter.default.removeObserver(self)
wasRunningBeforeBackground = false
onStateChange?("stopped")
onStateChange = nil
```

Also add a `deinit`:

```swift
deinit {
    NotificationCenter.default.removeObserver(self)
}
```

### Step 7: Create CameraStateStreamHandler.swift

Create `ios/Runner/CameraStateStreamHandler.swift`:

```swift
import Flutter

final class CameraStateStreamHandler: NSObject, FlutterStreamHandler {

    static let channelName = "com.mayasabhaxr.maya_viewer/camera_state"

    // Injected at init time — the pipeline whose state this handler streams
    private let pipeline: CameraCapturePipeline

    init(pipeline: CameraCapturePipeline) {
        self.pipeline = pipeline
    }

    func onListen(withArguments arguments: Any?,
                  eventSink events: @escaping FlutterEventSink) -> FlutterError? {
        pipeline.onStateChange = { stateString in
            DispatchQueue.main.async {
                events(stateString)
            }
        }
        return nil
    }

    func onCancel(withArguments arguments: Any?) -> FlutterError? {
        pipeline.onStateChange = nil
        return nil
    }
}
```

`CameraStateStreamHandler` must receive the **same** `CameraCapturePipeline`
instance that `CameraChannelHandler` holds — they must share one pipeline, not
each create their own.

### Step 8: Update AppDelegate.swift — share the pipeline instance

`CameraChannelHandler` currently owns a `CameraCapturePipeline` internally.
Refactor so the pipeline is constructed once in `AppDelegate` and injected:

In `CameraChannelHandler.swift`, change:
```swift
// BEFORE
private let pipeline = CameraCapturePipeline()

// AFTER
private let pipeline: CameraCapturePipeline
init(pipeline: CameraCapturePipeline) { self.pipeline = pipeline }
```

In `AppDelegate.swift`, replace the existing camera handler registration with:

```swift
let sharedPipeline    = CameraCapturePipeline()
let cameraHandler     = CameraChannelHandler(pipeline: sharedPipeline)
let cameraStateHandler = CameraStateStreamHandler(pipeline: sharedPipeline)

let cameraChannel = FlutterMethodChannel(
    name: CameraChannelHandler.channelName,
    binaryMessenger: controller.binaryMessenger
)
cameraChannel.setMethodCallHandler(cameraHandler.handle)

let cameraStateChannel = FlutterEventChannel(
    name: CameraStateStreamHandler.channelName,
    binaryMessenger: controller.binaryMessenger
)
cameraStateChannel.setStreamHandler(cameraStateHandler)
```

Store all four (`sharedPipeline`, `cameraHandler`, `cameraStateHandler`,
`cameraStateChannel`) as strong `AppDelegate` instance properties.

### Step 9: Create lib/channels/camera_state_channel.dart

```dart
import 'package:flutter/services.dart';

const _channel = EventChannel('com.mayasabhaxr.maya_viewer/camera_state');

/// Emits one of: "running" | "suspended" | "interrupted" | "resumed" |
/// "stopped" | "error"
Stream<String> get cameraStateStream =>
    _channel.receiveBroadcastStream().map((event) => event as String);
```

## API / Data Contract

EventChannel name: `com.mayasabhaxr.maya_viewer/camera_state`

Payload per event: `String` — one of the following state tokens:

| Token         | Meaning                                                       |
|---------------|---------------------------------------------------------------|
| `"running"`   | Session active; captures are possible                        |
| `"suspended"` | Paused on background entry; will auto-resume on foreground   |
| `"interrupted"` | System claimed camera (call, FaceID, Siri, etc.)           |
| `"resumed"`   | Returned from interrupted state; session restarted           |
| `"stopped"`   | Explicitly stopped by Flutter via `stopSession()`            |
| `"error"`     | Unrecoverable session error; Flutter should call `startSession` to retry |

No error objects are emitted on this channel — only the string tokens above.
`FlutterError` is reserved for the MethodChannel.

## Analytics Events

Event: `camera_session_interrupted`
Trigger: when `onStateChange?("interrupted")` fires inside
`handleInterruption`
Properties:
  - `interruption_reason`: String — one of `"audioDeviceInUseByAnotherClient"`,
    `"videoDeviceInUseByAnotherClient"`, `"videoDeviceNotAvailableInBackground"`,
    `"videoDeviceNotAvailableWithMultipleForegroundApps"`, `"unknown"`
    (derive from `AVCaptureSession.InterruptionReason` raw value)
  - `session_was_running`: Bool — `true` if the session was active at the
    moment of interruption

Event: `camera_session_error`
Trigger: when `onStateChange?("error")` fires inside `handleRuntimeError`
Properties:
  - `error_code`: Int — `AVError.code.rawValue`
  - `auto_recovered`: Bool — `true` if the `.mediaServicesWereReset` restart
    path was taken

## What NOT to Change

- Do NOT modify `BlurMethodChannelHandler.swift` or `OrientationStreamHandler.swift`
- Do NOT add any background mode entitlement (`UIBackgroundModes`) to
  `Info.plist` — the correct approach is to pause the session on background,
  not request a background entitlement
- Do NOT change the `com.mayasabhaxr.maya_viewer/camera` MethodChannel name,
  its registered methods (`startSession`, `stopSession`, `capturePhoto`), or
  their return types
- Do NOT call `session.stopRunning()` or `session.startRunning()` from the
  main thread — all session operations must stay on `sessionQueue`
- Do NOT import Flutter into `CameraCapturePipeline.swift` — the `onStateChange`
  closure bridge preserves the zero-Flutter-import constraint
- Do NOT modify `lib/channels/camera_channel.dart` or
  `lib/channels/blur_channel.dart`
- Do NOT add a second `CameraCapturePipeline` instance — `AppDelegate` must
  construct exactly one and inject it into both handlers

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] App is backgrounded while a `capturePhoto` is in-flight →
  `session.stopRunning()` is called; the in-flight `AVCapturePhoto` delegate
  callback will still fire (AVFoundation guarantees this); the completion must
  succeed or fail normally — do NOT cancel in-flight captures on background
- [ ] `willEnterForeground` fires but `wasRunningBeforeBackground` is `false`
  (Flutter called `stopSession()` before backgrounding) → do NOT restart the
  session; the guard in `handleWillEnterForeground` must prevent this
- [ ] Two rapid background/foreground cycles before `sessionQueue` processes
  the first → `wasRunningBeforeBackground` may be set/cleared out of order;
  serialised `sessionQueue` guarantees FIFO execution, so this is safe by
  construction — add a `#if DEBUG` assertion to confirm
- [ ] `AVCaptureSessionRuntimeError` with `.mediaServicesWereReset` → attempt
  one automatic `startRunning`; emit `"running"` if it succeeds; if
  `session.isRunning` is still false after the restart attempt, emit `"error"`
- [ ] `onStateChange` is `nil` when a state transition fires (Dart has not yet
  subscribed or has cancelled) → the optional call `onStateChange?(...)` is a
  no-op; do NOT queue unsent events for replay — Dart must subscribe before
  starting the session to receive all transitions
- [ ] `deinit` fires while observers are still registered (e.g. force-quit) →
  `NotificationCenter.default.removeObserver(self)` in `deinit` handles this;
  confirm it does not double-remove (harmless, but confirm no crash)

## Constraints

- `NotificationCenter` observers must use the closure-based
  `addObserver(forName:object:queue:using:)` overload, NOT
  `addObserver(_:selector:name:object:)` — the selector overload requires
  `@objc` on private methods, which conflicts with the `final class` convention
  used in this codebase
- All observer closures must capture `self` as `[weak self]` — strong capture
  creates a retain cycle between the pipeline and `NotificationCenter`
- `UIApplication.didEnterBackgroundNotification` must be observed on the
  Swift side only — do NOT route app lifecycle through Flutter's
  `WidgetsBindingObserver` for camera session management; the native observer
  fires earlier and on the correct thread
- The `onStateChange` closure is assigned and cleared only from
  `CameraStateStreamHandler` (on the main thread via `onListen`/`onCancel`)
  and read only from `sessionQueue`. This cross-queue access to a closure
  property is safe because closure assignment is atomic on 64-bit ARM; do NOT
  add a lock — it would risk deadlock with `sessionQueue`
- `wasRunningBeforeBackground` is accessed exclusively on `sessionQueue`;
  do NOT read or write it from any other queue

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Starting the session, then pressing the home button, then returning to
  the app: the session is `"suspended"` within 1 second of backgrounding and
  `"running"` within 2 seconds of foregrounding — confirmed via `cameraStateStream`
  log in a debug screen on a physical iPhone
- [ ] Calling `stopSession()` from Flutter, then backgrounding and foregrounding:
  the session does NOT auto-restart on foreground; `cameraStateStream` emits
  `"stopped"` exactly once and nothing further
- [ ] Receiving an incoming phone call while the session is running: the stream
  emits `"interrupted"`, then `"resumed"` after the call is dismissed — no crash,
  no `SESSION_NOT_STARTED` error on the next `capturePhoto`
- [ ] A `capturePhoto` in-flight at the moment of backgrounding completes
  successfully (returns a valid file path) or fails with `CAPTURE_FAILED` —
  it never hangs indefinitely (verify with a 10-second timeout in the test)
- [ ] Rapid home-button → app-switch × 5 in 3 seconds does not crash the app
  and leaves the session in `"running"` state after the final foreground return
- [ ] `NotificationCenter` has zero observers from `CameraCapturePipeline`
  after `stopSession()` is called — verified by setting a breakpoint in
  `deinit` or by checking `NotificationCenter.default` observer count in a
  debug build
- [ ] The `camera_session_interrupted` analytics event fires with a non-empty
  `interruption_reason` string on a real phone call interruption
- [ ] `flutter build ios --no-codesign` compiles without warnings
- [ ] `dart analyze` reports zero new issues

## Testing Instructions

1. **Background/foreground cycle** (physical device required):
   Start the session via `startCameraSession()`. Press home. Watch
   `cameraStateStream` — confirm `"suspended"` appears within 1s. Return to
   app — confirm `"running"` appears within 2s. Call `capturePhoto` — confirm
   a valid file path is returned.

2. **Explicit stop then background**:
   Start session, call `stopCameraSession()`, confirm `"stopped"` on stream.
   Background and foreground the app. Confirm no additional state event fires
   and `isRunning` on the session is `false` (check in Xcode debugger).

3. **Phone call interruption** (physical device, use a second phone to call):
   Start session. Receive call, decline. Confirm `"interrupted"` then
   `"resumed"` on `cameraStateStream`. Call `capturePhoto` after resume —
   confirm success.

4. **In-flight capture during backgrounding**:
   Trigger `capturePhoto`. Immediately press home (within ~200ms). Confirm
   either a valid path or `CAPTURE_FAILED` is returned to Dart — never a hang.
   Use a 10-second `Future.timeout` in the test to detect hangs.

5. **Rapid background/foreground stress**:
   In a loop, background and foreground the app 5 times in 3 seconds using
   the Xcode debugger's `simulate memory warning` + home button. Confirm no
   crash in Xcode console and final state is `"running"`.

6. **Observer cleanup**:
   Call `startSession`, then `stopSession`. In Xcode, pause execution and
   inspect `NotificationCenter.default` — confirm no entries pointing to
   `CameraCapturePipeline`.

7. **Analytics**:
   Trigger a phone-call interruption. Confirm `camera_session_interrupted`
   event is logged with a valid `interruption_reason` and
   `session_was_running: true`.

8. **Build**:
   `flutter build ios --no-codesign` and `dart analyze` — both must pass with
   zero new warnings or errors.

## Assumptions

- Assumed: The app does NOT declare `UIBackgroundModes` containing `"camera"`
  or `"voip"` in `Info.plist` — if it does, `didEnterBackgroundNotification`
  behaviour changes and this implementation needs review before merging.
- Assumed: `CameraChannelHandler` currently instantiates `CameraCapturePipeline`
  privately (`private let pipeline = CameraCapturePipeline()`). Step 8
  refactors this to constructor injection. If the pipeline is already injected
  or shared via another mechanism, skip Step 8 and wire `CameraStateStreamHandler`
  to the existing shared instance directly.
- Assumed: The minimum iOS deployment target is iOS 13.0 or later —
  `AVCaptureSession.InterruptionReason` and the closure-based
  `NotificationCenter.addObserver` API are available on iOS 9+, so this is
  not a constraint in practice, but confirm before merging if the target is
  lower than iOS 13.

  Generate a coding prompt for this task: FileManager local project folder management — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: iOS Native (Swift)

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------>>>>>>

# FEATURE: FileManager Local Project Folder Management
# Product: maya-viewer (iOS Native)
# Scope: New Feature
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Implement a local project folder management layer on the iOS Swift side using
`FileManager` that creates, lists, reads, and deletes named project folders
inside the app's `Documents` directory. All file operations are exposed to
Flutter via a single MethodChannel. No cloud sync, no `PHPhotoLibrary` — local
filesystem only.

- [ ] Create `ProjectStorageManager.swift` with methods to: create a project
  folder by ID, save a file (as `Data`) into a project folder, list all files
  in a project folder, read a file as `Data` from a project folder, delete a
  single file, delete an entire project folder, and list all existing project
  folder IDs
- [ ] All paths are derived deterministically from a `projectId` string and a
  `filename` string — callers never pass raw file paths
- [ ] Expose all operations via MethodChannel
  `com.mayasabhaxr.maya_viewer/storage` with the method set defined in the
  API contract below
- [ ] Create `StorageChannelHandler.swift` to own the Flutter ↔ Swift bridge;
  it holds a `ProjectStorageManager` instance and dispatches all operations to
  a dedicated background serial queue
- [ ] Register the MethodChannel in `AppDelegate.swift` following the existing
  handler registration pattern
- [ ] Create `lib/channels/storage_channel.dart` on the Dart side
- [ ] All file I/O must occur on a dedicated background queue — never on the
  main thread; all `FlutterResult` callbacks must be dispatched back to the
  main thread before calling `result(...)`

## Files to Inspect First

1. `ios/Runner/AppDelegate.swift` — identify where existing MethodChannel and
   EventChannel handlers are stored as strong instance properties; mirror the
   exact registration pattern for the new storage channel
2. `ios/Runner/CameraChannelHandler.swift` — use as the structural template
   for `StorageChannelHandler.swift` (method dispatch pattern, error code
   style, `FlutterResult` + `DispatchQueue.main.async` usage)
3. `ios/Runner/BlurMethodChannelHandler.swift` — confirm the convention for
   validating `call.arguments` as `[String: Any]` before use; apply the same
   guard pattern in `StorageChannelHandler`
4. `lib/channels/camera_channel.dart` — use as the template for
   `storage_channel.dart` (channel name constant placement, `invokeMethod`
   return type style, `PlatformException` propagation)

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Create ProjectStorageManager.swift

Create `ios/Runner/ProjectStorageManager.swift`. This file has zero Flutter
imports — all Flutter wiring lives in `StorageChannelHandler.swift`.

The root directory for all project data is:
`<Documents>/maya_projects/<projectId>/`

```swift
import Foundation

final class ProjectStorageManager {

    // MARK: - Root

    /// Returns the `maya_projects` root inside the app's Documents directory.
    /// Creates it if absent.
    private func projectsRoot() throws -> URL {
        let docs = try FileManager.default.url(
            for: .documentDirectory,
            in: .userDomainMask,
            appropriateFor: nil,
            create: true
        )
        let root = docs.appendingPathComponent("maya_projects", isDirectory: true)
        try FileManager.default.createDirectory(
            at: root, withIntermediateDirectories: true)
        return root
    }

    /// Returns the folder URL for a given projectId. Does NOT create it.
    private func projectFolder(for projectId: String) throws -> URL {
        try projectsRoot().appendingPathComponent(
            sanitise(projectId), isDirectory: true)
    }

    // MARK: - Public API

    /// Creates the project folder. Idempotent — succeeds if it already exists.
    func createProject(projectId: String) throws {
        let folder = try projectFolder(for: projectId)
        try FileManager.default.createDirectory(
            at: folder, withIntermediateDirectories: true)
    }

    /// Writes `data` to `<projectFolder>/<filename>`. Creates the project
    /// folder if absent. Overwrites silently if the file already exists.
    func saveFile(projectId: String, filename: String, data: Data) throws {
        try createProject(projectId: projectId)
        let url = try projectFolder(for: projectId)
            .appendingPathComponent(sanitise(filename))
        try data.write(to: url, options: .atomic)
    }

    /// Returns a list of filenames (not full paths) inside the project folder.
    /// Returns an empty array if the project folder does not exist.
    func listFiles(projectId: String) throws -> [String] {
        let folder = try projectFolder(for: projectId)
        guard FileManager.default.fileExists(atPath: folder.path) else {
            return []
        }
        return try FileManager.default.contentsOfDirectory(atPath: folder.path)
    }

    /// Returns the raw bytes of the named file inside the project folder.
    func readFile(projectId: String, filename: String) throws -> Data {
        let url = try projectFolder(for: projectId)
            .appendingPathComponent(sanitise(filename))
        guard FileManager.default.fileExists(atPath: url.path) else {
            throw StorageError.fileNotFound(filename)
        }
        return try Data(contentsOf: url)
    }

    /// Deletes a single file. Throws `fileNotFound` if absent.
    func deleteFile(projectId: String, filename: String) throws {
        let url = try projectFolder(for: projectId)
            .appendingPathComponent(sanitise(filename))
        guard FileManager.default.fileExists(atPath: url.path) else {
            throw StorageError.fileNotFound(filename)
        }
        try FileManager.default.removeItem(at: url)
    }

    /// Deletes the entire project folder and all its contents. Idempotent —
    /// succeeds silently if the folder does not exist.
    func deleteProject(projectId: String) throws {
        let folder = try projectFolder(for: projectId)
        guard FileManager.default.fileExists(atPath: folder.path) else { return }
        try FileManager.default.removeItem(at: folder)
    }

    /// Returns all project IDs (folder names) under `maya_projects/`.
    func listProjects() throws -> [String] {
        let root = try projectsRoot()
        return try FileManager.default.contentsOfDirectory(atPath: root.path)
    }

    // MARK: - Helpers

    enum StorageError: Error {
        case fileNotFound(String)
        case invalidProjectId
        case invalidFilename
    }

    /// Strips path separators and null bytes to prevent path traversal.
    /// Throws `invalidProjectId` / `invalidFilename` on empty or traversal
    /// input — callers label the error accordingly.
    private func sanitise(_ component: String) throws -> String {
        let cleaned = component
            .replacingOccurrences(of: "/",  with: "_")
            .replacingOccurrences(of: "\\", with: "_")
            .replacingOccurrences(of: "\0", with: "")
            .trimmingCharacters(in: .whitespacesAndNewlines)
        guard !cleaned.isEmpty, cleaned != "..", cleaned != "." else {
            throw StorageError.invalidProjectId   // caller remaps to the right error
        }
        return cleaned
    }
}
```

### Step 2: Create StorageChannelHandler.swift

Create `ios/Runner/StorageChannelHandler.swift`. This file is the only file
that imports Flutter in this feature:

```swift
import Flutter

final class StorageChannelHandler {

    static let channelName = "com.mayasabhaxr.maya_viewer/storage"

    private let storage = ProjectStorageManager()
    private let ioQueue = DispatchQueue(
        label: "com.mayasabhaxr.storage.io", qos: .userInitiated)

    func handle(call: FlutterMethodCall, result: @escaping FlutterResult) {
        // Dispatch all I/O to background; return to main for FlutterResult
        ioQueue.async { [weak self] in
            guard let self else { return }
            do {
                let output = try self.dispatch(call: call)
                DispatchQueue.main.async { result(output) }
            } catch let e as ProjectStorageManager.StorageError {
                DispatchQueue.main.async {
                    result(FlutterError(
                        code: self.errorCode(for: e),
                        message: e.localizedDescription,
                        details: nil))
                }
            } catch {
                DispatchQueue.main.async {
                    result(FlutterError(
                        code: "IO_ERROR",
                        message: error.localizedDescription,
                        details: nil))
                }
            }
        }
    }

    // MARK: - Dispatch

    private func dispatch(call: FlutterMethodCall) throws -> Any? {
        let args = call.arguments as? [String: Any]

        switch call.method {

        case "createProject":
            let id = try require(args?["projectId"] as? String,
                                 code: "INVALID_ARGS", field: "projectId")
            try storage.createProject(projectId: id)
            return nil

        case "saveFile":
            let id   = try require(args?["projectId"] as? String,
                                   code: "INVALID_ARGS", field: "projectId")
            let name = try require(args?["filename"] as? String,
                                   code: "INVALID_ARGS", field: "filename")
            let raw  = try require(
                (args?["bytes"] as? FlutterStandardTypedData)?.data,
                code: "INVALID_ARGS", field: "bytes")
            try storage.saveFile(projectId: id, filename: name, data: raw)
            return nil

        case "listFiles":
            let id = try require(args?["projectId"] as? String,
                                 code: "INVALID_ARGS", field: "projectId")
            return try storage.listFiles(projectId: id)

        case "readFile":
            let id   = try require(args?["projectId"] as? String,
                                   code: "INVALID_ARGS", field: "projectId")
            let name = try require(args?["filename"] as? String,
                                   code: "INVALID_ARGS", field: "filename")
            let data = try storage.readFile(projectId: id, filename: name)
            return FlutterStandardTypedData(bytes: data)

        case "deleteFile":
            let id   = try require(args?["projectId"] as? String,
                                   code: "INVALID_ARGS", field: "projectId")
            let name = try require(args?["filename"] as? String,
                                   code: "INVALID_ARGS", field: "filename")
            try storage.deleteFile(projectId: id, filename: name)
            return nil

        case "deleteProject":
            let id = try require(args?["projectId"] as? String,
                                 code: "INVALID_ARGS", field: "projectId")
            try storage.deleteProject(projectId: id)
            return nil

        case "listProjects":
            return try storage.listProjects()

        default:
            return FlutterMethodNotImplemented
        }
    }

    // MARK: - Helpers

    private struct ArgError: Error {
        let code: String; let field: String
        var localizedDescription: String { "Missing or invalid argument: \(field)" }
    }

    private func require<T>(_ value: T?, code: String, field: String) throws -> T {
        guard let v = value else { throw ArgError(code: code, field: field) }
        return v
    }

    private func errorCode(
        for error: ProjectStorageManager.StorageError) -> String {
        switch error {
        case .fileNotFound:    return "FILE_NOT_FOUND"
        case .invalidProjectId: return "INVALID_PROJECT_ID"
        case .invalidFilename:  return "INVALID_FILENAME"
        }
    }
}
```

### Step 3: Register in AppDelegate.swift

In `AppDelegate.swift`, inside `application(_:didFinishLaunchingWithOptions:)`,
after the existing camera/blur/orientation registrations:

```swift
let storageHandler = StorageChannelHandler()
let storageChannel = FlutterMethodChannel(
    name: StorageChannelHandler.channelName,
    binaryMessenger: controller.binaryMessenger
)
storageChannel.setMethodCallHandler(storageHandler.handle)
```

Store `storageHandler` and `storageChannel` as strong `AppDelegate` instance
properties — do not declare them as local `let` variables.

### Step 4: Create lib/channels/storage_channel.dart

```dart
import 'dart:typed_data';
import 'package:flutter/services.dart';

const _channel = MethodChannel('com.mayasabhaxr.maya_viewer/storage');

Future<void> createProject(String projectId) =>
    _channel.invokeMethod('createProject', {'projectId': projectId});

Future<void> saveFile({
  required String projectId,
  required String filename,
  required Uint8List bytes,
}) =>
    _channel.invokeMethod('saveFile', {
      'projectId': projectId,
      'filename': filename,
      'bytes': bytes,
    });

Future<List<String>> listFiles(String projectId) async {
  final result = await _channel.invokeListMethod<String>(
      'listFiles', {'projectId': projectId});
  return result ?? [];
}

Future<Uint8List> readFile({
  required String projectId,
  required String filename,
}) async {
  final data = await _channel.invokeMethod<Uint8List>(
      'readFile', {'projectId': projectId, 'filename': filename});
  if (data == null) throw PlatformException(
      code: 'FILE_NOT_FOUND', message: 'readFile returned null');
  return data;
}

Future<void> deleteFile({
  required String projectId,
  required String filename,
}) =>
    _channel.invokeMethod('deleteFile', {
      'projectId': projectId,
      'filename': filename,
    });

Future<void> deleteProject(String projectId) =>
    _channel.invokeMethod('deleteProject', {'projectId': projectId});

Future<List<String>> listProjects() async {
  final result = await _channel.invokeListMethod<String>('listProjects');
  return result ?? [];
}
```

## API / Data Contract

MethodChannel name: `com.mayasabhaxr.maya_viewer/storage`

| Method          | Arguments (Map)                                        | Returns                          |
|-----------------|--------------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------|
| `createProject` | `projectId: String`                                    | `null`                           |
| `saveFile`      | `projectId: String`, `filename: String`, `bytes: FlutterStandardTypedData` | `null` |
| `listFiles`     | `projectId: String`                                    | `List<String>` (filenames only)  |
| `readFile`      | `projectId: String`, `filename: String`                | `FlutterStandardTypedData`       |
| `deleteFile`    | `projectId: String`, `filename: String`                | `null`                           |
| `deleteProject` | `projectId: String`                                    | `null`                           |
| `listProjects`  | _(none)_                                               | `List<String>` (project IDs)     |

Error codes returned via `FlutterError`:

| Code                  | Meaning                                              |
|-----------------------|------------------------------------------------------|
| `INVALID_ARGS`        | A required argument is missing or the wrong type     |
| `FILE_NOT_FOUND`      | `readFile` or `deleteFile` target does not exist     |
| `INVALID_PROJECT_ID`  | `projectId` is empty, `"."`, or `".."`               |
| `INVALID_FILENAME`    | `filename` is empty, `"."`, or `".."`                |
| `IO_ERROR`            | Underlying `FileManager` or `Data` I/O failure       |

## What NOT to Change

- Do NOT modify `CameraCapturePipeline.swift`, `CameraChannelHandler.swift`,
  `CameraStateStreamHandler.swift`, `BlurMethodChannelHandler.swift`, or
  `OrientationStreamHandler.swift`
- Do NOT touch any existing channel registrations in `AppDelegate.swift`
  beyond appending the new storage channel block at the end
- Do NOT use `NSFileCoordinator` or `NSFilePresenter` — these are required for
  iCloud Drive coordination only; this task is local-only
- Do NOT save to `NSTemporaryDirectory()` — project files must persist across
  app launches and live in `Documents/maya_projects/`; temp directory is for
  capture output only (owned by the camera pipeline)
- Do NOT expose raw `URL` or absolute path strings to Flutter — filenames and
  projectIds only cross the channel boundary; full paths stay in Swift
- Do NOT add iCloud entitlements or `NSUbiquitousContainers` to `Info.plist`
- Do NOT modify any existing Dart channel files

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] `projectId` or `filename` contains `/`, `\`, or null bytes → `sanitise()`
  replaces separators with `_` and strips nulls; the cleaned component is used;
  callers are NOT notified of the substitution — it is silent normalisation
- [ ] `projectId` or `filename` is `".."` or `"."` after sanitisation →
  throw `StorageError.invalidProjectId` / `invalidFilename`; return
  `INVALID_PROJECT_ID` / `INVALID_FILENAME` to Flutter
- [ ] `createProject` called when the folder already exists →
  `createDirectory(withIntermediateDirectories: true)` is idempotent; return
  `null` (success) without error
- [ ] `saveFile` called for a project that does not yet exist → `saveFile`
  calls `createProject` internally first; never return `FILE_NOT_FOUND` for
  a missing project on a save operation
- [ ] `listFiles` called for a non-existent project → return an empty
  `List<String>`, not an error
- [ ] `deleteProject` called for a non-existent project → return `null`
  (success), not `FILE_NOT_FOUND`
- [ ] `readFile` or `deleteFile` called for a file that does not exist →
  return `FlutterError(code: "FILE_NOT_FOUND")`
- [ ] Disk full during `saveFile` → `data.write(to:options:.atomic)` throws;
  propagate as `IO_ERROR`; the partial `.atomic` write is automatically
  discarded by the OS — no cleanup needed
- [ ] Two concurrent `saveFile` calls for the same path → `ioQueue` is a
  serial queue; operations are serialised by construction; no locking needed
  at the `ProjectStorageManager` level
- [ ] `listFiles` returns hidden files (`.DS_Store` on macOS simulator) →
  filter out any filename that starts with `"."` before returning to Flutter

## Constraints

- `ProjectStorageManager` must have zero Flutter imports — it must be unit-
  testable in isolation using `XCTestCase` without a Flutter engine
- All `FileManager` and `Data` I/O must execute on `ioQueue` inside
  `StorageChannelHandler` — `ProjectStorageManager` itself does not own a
  queue; it is the handler's responsibility to call it off the main thread
- `bytes` must cross the channel as `FlutterStandardTypedData` in both
  directions — do NOT encode as base64 String; `FlutterStandardTypedData`
  is zero-copy on the platform channel and is the correct type for binary blobs
- The `maya_projects` root folder name is a hardcoded constant in
  `ProjectStorageManager` — do NOT make it configurable via channel arguments;
  if it needs to change, it changes in one place in Swift only
- `sanitise()` must be applied to both `projectId` and `filename` at every
  call site inside `ProjectStorageManager` — never trust a raw string from the
  caller to construct a path component

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Calling `createProject("scan_001")` creates the folder
  `<Documents>/maya_projects/scan_001/` on device — verified by checking
  `Files` app (enable iTunes file sharing in Info.plist for testing, remove
  after)
- [ ] `saveFile(projectId: "scan_001", filename: "frame_01.heic", bytes: <validData>)`
  writes the file; subsequent `readFile` returns bytes with identical length
  and SHA-256 hash as the original data
- [ ] `listFiles("scan_001")` after saving three files returns exactly those
  three filenames; no hidden files (`.DS_Store`) appear in the list
- [ ] `listProjects()` after creating two projects returns both project IDs;
  after `deleteProject("scan_001")`, returns only the remaining one
- [ ] `readFile` for a non-existent filename returns
  `PlatformException(code: "FILE_NOT_FOUND")` in Dart — no crash
- [ ] `deleteProject` for a non-existent ID returns normally (no exception)
  in Dart
- [ ] `saveFile` with `projectId: "../etc"` silently normalises to
  `"..\_etc"` (separator replaced) and writes successfully — confirmed by
  checking the actual folder name created on disk
- [ ] `saveFile` with `projectId: ".."` (resolves to `".."` after sanitise)
  returns `PlatformException(code: "INVALID_PROJECT_ID")` — no path traversal
- [ ] All file I/O calls complete without blocking the Flutter UI thread —
  confirmed by running `saveFile` with a 5MB byte array and verifying the
  Flutter frame rate does not drop below 55fps during the call (use Flutter
  DevTools Performance tab)
- [ ] `flutter build ios --no-codesign` compiles without warnings
- [ ] `dart analyze` reports zero new issues

## Testing Instructions

1. **Create and read round-trip** (physical device or Simulator):
   Call `createProject("test_proj")`, `saveFile(...)` with a known 1KB
   `Uint8List`, then `readFile(...)`. Assert the returned bytes are identical
   to the input using `listEquals` or SHA-256 comparison in Dart.

2. **List files — hidden file filter**:
   On Simulator, create a project, save two files. Call `listFiles`. Assert
   exactly 2 filenames are returned and none start with `"."`.

3. **Delete project idempotency**:
   Call `deleteProject("nonexistent_id")`. Assert no exception is thrown.
   Call it a second time on the same ID after `createProject` + `deleteProject`.
   Assert still no exception.

4. **Path traversal guard**:
   Call `saveFile(projectId: "..", filename: "evil.txt", bytes: ...)`. Assert
   `PlatformException(code: "INVALID_PROJECT_ID")` is thrown in Dart. Confirm
   no file was created outside `maya_projects/` by listing `Documents/` root.

5. **Concurrent saves** (stress test):
   Fire 10 simultaneous `saveFile` calls for the same `projectId` but different
   filenames using `Future.wait`. Assert all 10 complete successfully and
   `listFiles` returns exactly 10 filenames.

6. **Performance test**:
   Call `saveFile` with a 5MB `Uint8List`. Measure wall time from Dart call
   to completion — must be under 3 seconds on an iPhone 12. Confirm Flutter
   UI thread is not blocked (DevTools shows no jank frame during the call).

7. **Build test**:
   `flutter build ios --no-codesign` and `dart analyze` — both must pass with
   zero new warnings or errors.

## Assumptions

- Assumed: iTunes file sharing (`UIFileSharingEnabled`) is not permanently
  enabled in `Info.plist` — it may be toggled on temporarily for QA file
  inspection during testing but must be removed before release.
- Assumed: The app does not use iCloud Drive or `NSUbiquitousItemIsUploadedKey`
  for project files — all storage is strictly local to the device. If iCloud
  sync is added later, `NSFileCoordinator` will need to wrap all operations in
  `ProjectStorageManager` and this prompt will need revisiting.
- Assumed: `projectId` and `filename` values passed from Dart are valid UTF-8
  strings — `FileManager` path components require valid Unicode; if binary or
  non-UTF-8 IDs are needed in future, a separate encoding layer is required.
- Assumed: The `bytes` argument in `saveFile` and the return value of
  `readFile` use `FlutterStandardTypedData` (binary) — if the team has
  standardised on base64 String encoding for binary data across other channels,
  align with that convention instead and update the Dart wrapper accordingly.

# NEW FEATURE: CaptureController — Camera Preview, Orientation Stream & Capture Trigger
# Product: maya-viewer (AR Advertising / Native Camera Pipeline)
# Scope: New Feature
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Implement `CaptureController` as a Riverpod `StateNotifier` (or `AsyncNotifier` if lifecycle
demands it) that owns the native camera pipeline for the Flutter Platform Bridge phase.
It must expose three reactive surfaces:

- [ ] **Camera preview stream** — a `StateProvider` or `StreamProvider` that yields
      `CameraPreviewWidget`-compatible data (via `camera` package `CameraController`) for
      rendering in the AR overlay UI
- [ ] **Device orientation stream** — a `StreamProvider<DeviceOrientation>` sourced from
      `native_device_orientation` (or `sensors_plus` gyro fallback) that emits on every
      physical device rotation
- [ ] **Capture trigger** — a callable method `Future<void> triggerCapture()` on the notifier
      that initiates still-image or frame capture, writes the result to a `StateProvider<CapturedFrame?>`,
      and emits a capture-complete event downstream

---

## Files to Inspect First

1. `lib/features/camera/` — check if directory exists; if not, create it as the home for all
   camera pipeline code
2. `lib/providers/` — understand existing Riverpod provider naming conventions and scoping patterns
3. `lib/main.dart` or `lib/app.dart` — understand `ProviderScope` placement to ensure
   `CaptureController` is correctly scoped (not re-created on navigation)
4. `pubspec.yaml` — confirm `camera`, `riverpod` / `flutter_riverpod`, and
   `native_device_orientation` or `sensors_plus` are listed; add if missing
5. `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml` and `ios/Runner/Info.plist` — confirm camera
   and microphone permissions are declared; add if missing

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Define the CapturedFrame value type

Create `lib/features/camera/captured_frame.dart`:

```dart
import 'dart:typed_data';

class CapturedFrame {
  final Uint8List bytes;
  final DateTime capturedAt;
  final DeviceOrientation orientation;

  const CapturedFrame({
    required this.bytes,
    required this.capturedAt,
    required this.orientation,
  });
}
```

### Step 2: Create CaptureController as an AsyncNotifier

Create `lib/features/camera/capture_controller.dart`.

The notifier must:
- Initialize a `CameraController` using the first back-facing camera from
  `availableCameras()`. Resolution preset: `ResolutionPreset.high`.
- Call `await _cameraController.initialize()` in `build()` (AsyncNotifier pattern)
  so the provider exposes `AsyncValue<CameraController>` — callers use `.when()` to
  show preview or loading state.
- Dispose `_cameraController` in `dispose()` — do not skip this or you will leak the
  camera on iOS.
- Expose `Future<void> triggerCapture()` that calls
  `_cameraController.takePicture()`, reads the file bytes, reads current orientation
  from the orientation provider, constructs a `CapturedFrame`, and writes it to
  `capturedFrameProvider`.

```dart
final captureControllerProvider =
    AsyncNotifierProvider<CaptureController, CameraController>(
  CaptureController.new,
);
```

### Step 3: Create the orientation StreamProvider

Create `lib/features/camera/orientation_provider.dart`:

```dart
final deviceOrientationProvider = StreamProvider<DeviceOrientation>((ref) {
  return NativeDeviceOrientationCommunicator()
      .onOrientationChanged(useSensor: true);
});
```

If `native_device_orientation` is unavailable, fall back to
`sensors_plus` accelerometer stream and map to `DeviceOrientation` enum manually.
Document the fallback path with a `// FALLBACK:` comment.

### Step 4: Create the CapturedFrame StateProvider

In `lib/features/camera/capture_controller.dart` (same file, bottom):

```dart
final capturedFrameProvider = StateProvider<CapturedFrame?>((ref) => null);
```

### Step 5: Wire triggerCapture() to capturedFrameProvider

Inside `CaptureController.triggerCapture()`:

```dart
Future<void> triggerCapture() async {
  final xFile = await _cameraController.takePicture();
  final bytes = await xFile.readAsBytes();
  final orientation = ref.read(deviceOrientationProvider).valueOrNull
      ?? DeviceOrientation.portraitUp;
  ref.read(capturedFrameProvider.notifier).state = CapturedFrame(
    bytes: bytes,
    capturedAt: DateTime.now(),
    orientation: orientation,
  );
}
```

### Step 6: Expose CameraPreview in the UI layer

In the widget that renders the camera (do NOT create a new widget — locate the existing
AR overlay widget and add the preview there):

```dart
final cameraController = ref.watch(captureControllerProvider);
return cameraController.when(
  data: (controller) => CameraPreview(controller),
  loading: () => const CameraLoadingPlaceholder(),
  error: (e, _) => const CameraErrorPlaceholder(),
);
```

## What NOT to Change

- Do NOT modify any existing AR session initialization code — `CaptureController`
  is additive; it must not interfere with the AR pipeline
- Do NOT change the existing `ProviderScope` in `main.dart` — do not add overrides
  or restructure scope unless a test specifically requires it
- Do NOT touch the 3D model viewer or `maya-viewer` render loop
- Do NOT add any UI chrome (buttons, overlays, labels) in this task — only the
  controller and provider wiring; UI belongs in a separate task
- Do NOT dispose `CameraController` anywhere except `CaptureController.dispose()`

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] `availableCameras()` returns empty list → set state to `AsyncError` with
      `CameraNotFoundException`; surface `CameraErrorPlaceholder` in the UI
- [ ] `CameraController.initialize()` throws (permissions denied) → catch, set
      `AsyncError`, do not rethrow unhandled; log error via existing logger
- [ ] `triggerCapture()` called before controller is initialized → guard with
      `if (!_cameraController.value.isInitialized) return;`
- [ ] Device orientation stream emits null or errors → fall back silently to
      `DeviceOrientation.portraitUp`; never let orientation errors crash capture
- [ ] App goes to background mid-session → `CameraController.dispose()` must be called
      via `WidgetsBindingObserver.didChangeAppLifecycleState`; reconnect on resume

---

## Constraints

- `CaptureController` must be an `AsyncNotifier`, not a plain `StateNotifier` — the
  async initialization of `CameraController` makes `build()` inherently async
- The `captureControllerProvider` must be scoped at the root `ProviderScope` — do not
  scope it to a route or page widget or the controller will be recreated on navigation
- `CapturedFrame.bytes` must be `Uint8List`, not a file path — downstream AR pipeline
  consumers expect in-memory bytes
- Camera resolution must be `ResolutionPreset.high` — do not use `max` (causes OOM on
  low-end Android)
- All provider files must follow the existing Riverpod naming convention observed in
  `lib/providers/` (check before naming)

      the full camera init → preview → capture → dispose flow

  Generate a coding prompt for this task: Sensor stream parser: convert native EventChannel data → dart SensorFrame model — Phase: Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline, Track: Flutter Platform Bridge

  # NEW FEATURE: Sensor Stream Parser — EventChannel → SensorFrame Model
# Product: maya-viewer (AR Advertising / Native Camera & Sensor Pipeline)
# Scope: New Feature
# Priority: High

---

## Task Description

Implement a Dart-side sensor stream parser that subscribes to a native `EventChannel`,
receives raw sensor payloads from the platform layer (Android/iOS), and converts each
event into a typed `SensorFrame` Dart model. This is a pure data pipeline layer — no UI,
no Riverpod providers, no business logic beyond parsing and validation.

- [ ] Define the `SensorFrame` immutable value type with all required sensor fields
- [ ] Define a `SensorParseException` for structured parse error reporting
- [ ] Implement `SensorStreamParser` — a class that wraps the `EventChannel` and exposes
      a `Stream<SensorFrame>` with per-event parsing, validation, and error isolation
- [ ] Write unit tests covering valid payloads, missing fields, malformed types, and
      out-of-range values

---

## Files to Inspect First

1. `lib/features/camera/` — confirm directory exists (created in CaptureController task);
   place all new sensor parser files here under `lib/features/camera/sensor/`
2. `lib/features/camera/captured_frame.dart` — understand the `CapturedFrame` value type
   pattern to maintain consistency in `SensorFrame` design
3. `android/app/src/main/kotlin/` (or `.java/`) — locate the native plugin or method
   channel handler to confirm the exact channel name string and the raw Map structure
   being emitted on the Android side
4. `ios/Runner/` — locate the Swift/ObjC EventChannel registration to confirm the iOS
   channel name matches Android and inspect the raw dictionary structure it emits
5. `test/` — understand existing test file conventions (naming, folder structure,
   import style) before writing new unit tests

---

## Implementation Instructions

### Step 1: Define the SensorFrame model

Create `lib/features/camera/sensor/sensor_frame.dart`:

The raw EventChannel payload is a `Map<dynamic, dynamic>` with these keys (confirmed
from native side — do NOT assume additional keys exist):
{

"timestamp_us": int,        // microseconds since epoch

"accel_x": double,

"accel_y": double,

"accel_z": double,          // accelerometer in m/s²

"gyro_x": double,

"gyro_y": double,

"gyro_z": double,           // gyroscope in rad/s

"rotation_matrix": List<double>,  // 9-element row-major 3×3 matrix, nullable

}

Define `SensorFrame` as an immutable class (use `@immutable` annotation):

```dart
import 'package:flutter/foundation.dart';

@immutable
class SensorFrame {
  final int timestampUs;
  final double accelX;
  final double accelY;
  final double accelZ;
  final double gyroX;
  final double gyroY;
  final double gyroZ;
  final List<double>? rotationMatrix; // null if native did not emit it

  const SensorFrame({
    required this.timestampUs,
    required this.accelX,
    required this.accelY,
    required this.accelZ,
    required this.gyroX,
    required this.gyroY,
    required this.gyroZ,
    this.rotationMatrix,
  });
}
```

Do NOT add `toJson` / `fromJson` in this task — serialization is out of scope.

### Step 2: Define SensorParseException

In the same file `sensor_frame.dart`, below the class definition:

```dart
class SensorParseException implements Exception {
  final String message;
  final Map<dynamic, dynamic>? rawPayload;

  const SensorParseException(this.message, {this.rawPayload});

  @override
  String toString() =>
      'SensorParseException: $message | raw: $rawPayload';
}
```

### Step 3: Implement SensorStreamParser

Create `lib/features/camera/sensor/sensor_stream_parser.dart`:

```dart
import 'package:flutter/services.dart';
import 'sensor_frame.dart';

class SensorStreamParser {
  static const _channelName = 'com.mayasabhaxr.sensor/stream';

  final EventChannel _channel;

  SensorStreamParser({EventChannel? channel})
      : _channel = channel ??
            const EventChannel(_channelName);

  Stream<SensorFrame> get frames => _channel
      .receiveBroadcastStream()
      .map(_parseEvent)
      .handleError(_onChannelError);

  SensorFrame _parseEvent(dynamic event) {
    if (event is! Map) {
      throw SensorParseException(
        'Expected Map, got ${event.runtimeType}',
        rawPayload: null,
      );
    }
    final raw = Map<dynamic, dynamic>.from(event as Map);
    return _toFrame(raw);
  }

  SensorFrame _toFrame(Map<dynamic, dynamic> raw) {
    try {
      final rotationRaw = raw['rotation_matrix'];
      List<double>? rotationMatrix;
      if (rotationRaw != null) {
        final list = List<dynamic>.from(rotationRaw as List);
        if (list.length != 9) {
          throw SensorParseException(
            'rotation_matrix must have 9 elements, got ${list.length}',
            rawPayload: raw,
          );
        }
        rotationMatrix = list.map((e) => (e as num).toDouble()).toList();
      }

      return SensorFrame(
        timestampUs: _requireInt(raw, 'timestamp_us'),
        accelX: _requireDouble(raw, 'accel_x'),
        accelY: _requireDouble(raw, 'accel_y'),
        accelZ: _requireDouble(raw, 'accel_z'),
        gyroX: _requireDouble(raw, 'gyro_x'),
        gyroY: _requireDouble(raw, 'gyro_y'),
        gyroZ: _requireDouble(raw, 'gyro_z'),
        rotationMatrix: rotationMatrix,
      );
    } on SensorParseException {
      rethrow;
    } catch (e) {
      throw SensorParseException(
        'Unexpected parse error: $e',
        rawPayload: raw,
      );
    }
  }

  int _requireInt(Map<dynamic, dynamic> raw, String key) {
    final val = raw[key];
    if (val == null) throw SensorParseException('Missing key: $key', rawPayload: raw);
    if (val is int) return val;
    if (val is num) return val.toInt();
    throw SensorParseException(
        'Expected int for $key, got ${val.runtimeType}', rawPayload: raw);
  }

  double _requireDouble(Map<dynamic, dynamic> raw, String key) {
    final val = raw[key];
    if (val == null) throw SensorParseException('Missing key: $key', rawPayload: raw);
    if (val is double) return val;
    if (val is num) return val.toDouble();
    throw SensorParseException(
        'Expected double for $key, got ${val.runtimeType}', rawPayload: raw);
  }

  void _onChannelError(Object error, StackTrace stack) {
    // Errors from the native EventChannel (e.g. PlatformException) propagate
    // downstream to stream subscribers — do NOT swallow them here.
    // Callers decide whether to log, retry, or surface to UI.
  }
}
```

The `EventChannel` constructor is injected so unit tests can supply a mock channel
without touching the platform layer.

### Step 4: Write unit tests

Create `test/features/camera/sensor/sensor_stream_parser_test.dart`.

Use `StreamController<dynamic>` to simulate the EventChannel broadcast stream.
Mock `EventChannel.receiveBroadcastStream()` by injecting a fake channel whose
`receiveBroadcastStream` returns the controller's stream.

Test cases to cover — implement ALL of them:

1. **Valid full payload** → `SensorFrame` fields match input values exactly
2. **Valid payload with null rotation_matrix** → `SensorFrame.rotationMatrix` is null
3. **Missing required key (`accel_x` absent)** → `SensorParseException` is thrown with
   message containing `"Missing key: accel_x"`
4. **Wrong type for `timestamp_us` (String instead of int)** → `SensorParseException`
   thrown, not a `TypeError`
5. **`rotation_matrix` with 7 elements instead of 9** → `SensorParseException` thrown
   with message containing `"9 elements"`
6. **Non-Map event (e.g. raw String arrives)** → `SensorParseException` thrown, stream
   does not crash — subsequent valid events are still parsed correctly
7. **Multiple sequential valid events** → stream emits all frames in order, no frames
   dropped

---

## API / Data Contract

Native → Dart EventChannel payload (raw `Map<dynamic, dynamic>`):
{

"timestamp_us": int,          // required — microseconds since Unix epoch

"accel_x": double | int,      // required — m/s², coerced to double if int

"accel_y": double | int,      // required

"accel_z": double | int,      // required

"gyro_x": double | int,       // required — rad/s

"gyro_y": double | int,       // required

"gyro_z": double | int,       // required

"rotation_matrix": List<num>, // optional — null if sensor unavailable;

//   if present, must have exactly 9 elements

}

Any key not in this list must be silently ignored — do not throw for unknown keys.
This allows the native side to add future fields without breaking the parser.

## Edge Cases to Handle

- [ ] Native emits an `int` for a field declared as `double` (common on Android) →
      coerce via `.toDouble()`, never throw; `_requireDouble` already handles this —
      verify the implementation covers `num` supertype, not just `double`
